# Changelog

All notable changes to this project will be documented in this file.

The format is based on [Keep a Changelog](https://keepachangelog.com/en/1.0.0/),
and this project adheres to [Semantic Versioning](https://semver.org/spec/v2.0.0.html).

## [Unreleased]

### Changed
- **Mux tree observer delivery is deferred past the tree lock** (`crates/par-mux/src/mux/tree/`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/pane.rs`, `crates/par-term-emu-core/src/pty_session/io.rs`): resize-driven and start-dir-note observer events raised while a `SessionTree` method holds the tree mutex are now parked in `MuxTree::pending_observer_batches` and delivered by `take_observer_batches()` (new public method) after the lock drops — `dispatch_command`, reap, client teardown, and restore all drain it. Direct `MuxTree` callers (a program owning a bare `MuxTree`, not going through the daemon) no longer receive resize events inline: call `take_observer_batches()` where the old inline delivery was relied on. `PtySession` gains `#[doc(hidden)]` `resize_deferred`/`resize_with_pixels_deferred` behind the `mux` feature; the delivering `resize` wrappers are unchanged. The gone-start-dir note a spawn writes into its new pane (`spawn_and_wire`, under the tree lock) goes through the same path, so an observer a pane factory attaches in `create_pane` no longer runs under the tree mutex; `MuxPane::write_note` keeps its signature and now delivers after the terminal's write lock drops. The gone-cwd note restore (`MuxTree::from_persist_state`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/persist.rs`) writes into a restored pane follows suit: its observer events were delivered inline under that pane's terminal write lock and are now parked with the re-fit batches and delivered when restore drains them, before it returns. Delivery order across concurrent commands may interleave where the tree lock previously ordered it. Pinned by `layout_resize_observer_events_arrive_after_the_tree_lock_drops`, `a_direct_tree_resize_parks_observer_events_until_taken`, `start_dir_note_observer_events_arrive_after_the_tree_lock_drops`, and `restore_note_observer_events_arrive_after_the_terminal_lock_drops`; the core dac90e5 lock-discipline regression tests still pass.
- **The terminal core is now the `par-term-emu-core` workspace member** (`crates/par-term-emu-core/`, `Cargo.toml`, `src/lib.rs`, `cbindgen.toml`, ARC-007 option O2, phase 1): `terminal`, `grid`, `pty_session`, `graphics`, `screenshot`, `tmux_control`, `keyboard`, `macros`, `badge`, `coprocess`, `debug`, and the cell/color/cursor/mouse/zone/text/unicode utilities moved (by `git mv`, token-identical apart from the cross-boundary edits listed in the member's `DESIGN.md`) into a new crate that the root re-exports module by module. **No public path changes**: every `par_term_emu_core_rust::…` path, the `debug_*!` macros, the Python `_native` module and its stub, and `include/terminal_core.h` are unchanged. The member without features is the headless (`sim`) profile; root features `screenshot`, `pty_session`, `serde`, `macro-yaml`, `mux`, `ffi`, `python`/`python-test` forward to it. It versions in lockstep with the root (`make core-version-check`, in `checkall`/`release-check`/CI) and must be published to crates.io before the root crate (publish-workflow step still to add before the next release). Embedders that name only `par-term-emu-core-rust` need no change; pure-Rust embedders may depend on `par-term-emu-core` directly for the smaller build.
- **The multiplexer is now the `par-mux` workspace member** (`crates/par-mux/`, `Cargo.toml`, `src/lib.rs`, ARC-007 option O2, phase 2): `mux` (incl. `mux::attach`), the `par-mux` binary, and the eleven mux integration suites moved by `git mv` into a new crate that the root re-exports as `par_term_emu_core_rust::mux` behind its `mux` feature, so every `par_term_emu_core_rust::mux::…` path is unchanged. Root `mux-bin`/`attach` forward to the member's features; par-mux versions in lockstep with the root and both pins are exact (`make core-version-check` now gates root → core, root → par-mux, and par-mux → core). `mux::build_stamp()` comes from the member's own build script. The 31 direct `log::` call sites in the mux tree now log under `par_mux::mux::…` instead of `par_term_emu_core_rust::mux::…` (only matters to a `RUST_LOG` filter naming the old module path). Publish order is derive → `par-term-emu-core` → `par-mux` → root.
- **BREAKING (install): `cargo install par-term-emu-core-rust --bin par-mux` no longer works** — the binary lives in the `par-mux` crate. Install it with `cargo install par-mux --features mux-bin,attach` (from source: `cargo install --path crates/par-mux --features mux-bin,attach`; build: `cargo build -p par-mux --bin par-mux --features mux-bin`). The GitHub Release `par-mux` archives are unaffected.
- **Each grid's evicted-zone buffer is capped at 10,000 zones** (`src/grid/zone.rs`): zones that scroll off the scrollback floor wait in a per-grid buffer until they become `ZoneScrolledOut` events, and nothing bounded that buffer. A grid that is never drained could grow it without limit, for example one embedded directly with no owning `Terminal`. `MAX_EVICTED_ZONES` (10,000, matching `MAX_TERMINAL_EVENTS`) now bounds it, dropping the oldest evicted zones first, so at most 10,000 `ZoneScrolledOut` events surface from one backlog. Pinned by `test_evicted_zone_buffer_is_capped_fifo`.
- **BREAKING (Rust API): `TmuxNotification::Continue` now carries the pane id** (`src/tmux_control.rs`, `src/mux/emit.rs`, `src/python_bindings/types/notification.rs`): tmux's `%continue %N` names the pane whose output resumes, and the parser dropped it. `Continue` is now `Continue { pane_id: String }`, matching `Pause { pane_id }`. Exhaustive matches over the (non-`#[non_exhaustive]`) enum and constructions need the new field: `Continue` becomes `Continue { .. }` or `Continue { pane_id }`. Python `TmuxNotification.pane_id` is now set for `continue` notifications (it was `None`); the type string is unchanged. Known consumer: par-term's `parser_bridge.rs` matches `CoreNotification::Continue` and needs the same one-token update when it adopts this version.
- **par-mux `new-workspace` names its auto-created first tab `1`** (`src/mux/tree/mod.rs`, `src/mux/tree/lifecycle.rs`, `src/mux/dispatch.rs`, `docs/MUX.md`): the first window used to inherit the workspace's name, so a workspace `dev` showed a tab also called `dev`. The session keeps the workspace's name; only the window is named `1`. `new-session` is unchanged (its first window still takes the session name). Pinned by the first-window assertion in `workspace_commands_dispatch_and_broadcast`.
- **par-mux attach render mode: the tab strip takes herdr's tab-bar look** (`src/mux/attach/tabs.rs`, `src/mux/attach/render.rs`, `tests/mux_attach.rs`, `docs/MUX.md`): every tab is now a solid block with two columns of padding either side of its name (was one), adjacent blocks sit exactly one column apart (was none), and the whole row carries a dark navy bar background. Colors are truecolor constants in one place in `tabs.rs`, measured from the reference rendering: bar `rgb(23,23,33)`, inactive block `rgb(43,44,60)` with muted text `rgb(97,101,123)` (was DIM text with no fill), active block `rgb(126,171,249)` with dark bold text (was indexed cyan), and the `…` markers and the right-pinned ` + ` paint muted on the bar. The three-stage truncation, the always-visible active tab, the side panel's lead offset, and the ` + ` reservation are unchanged; the stage-2 and stage-3 budgets now count the gaps and each marker's trailing gap. Hit-testing runs through the same layout: a click anywhere on a block, pads included, hits that tab, and gap and marker columns hit nothing. Every strip column/width pin moved to the new geometry (for example the second of `main`/`vim`/`build` now spans cols 9..16 with col 8 a gap), and new pins cover the gap and marker misses (`hit_test_misses_markers_and_their_gaps`) and the palette (`paint_draws_herdr_blocks_on_the_bar`).
- **par-mux client lifecycle notifications fire only for size-reporting clients** (`src/mux/server.rs`, `tests/mux_daemon.rs`, `docs/MUX.md`): `%client-attached` now goes out when a registered client first reports a size (`refresh-client -C`) instead of at registration, and `%client-left` is emitted only for clients that were announced. Every one-shot `par-mux <cmd>` invocation registers as a client on its first control command, so under the registration-time rule each poll by an agent broadcast an attached/left pair to every attached client and forced a status refresh. A client that never reports a size (a one-shot command, a hook-only connection) is now silent in both directions. The joining client still never hears its own attach, and teardown is unchanged (clean disconnect, socket EOF and eviction all still emit for announced clients). Pinned by `a_client_that_never_reports_a_size_causes_no_lifecycle_lines` and the silence assertion added to `client_attach_and_drop_broadcast_lifecycle_lines`.

### Added
- **Two-tier Rust API: a curated `prelude` module** (`src/prelude.rs`, `src/lib.rs`, `docs/RUST_USAGE.md`, ARC-005): `par_term_emu_core_rust::prelude` re-exports the core embedder surface (Terminal, Grid, Cell, Color, Cursor, the event/observer types, `TerminalGraphic`, `TmuxNotification`, width config, and `pty_session`), with docs directing peripheral types to module-scoped paths. Every existing crate-root re-export is kept, so no consumer breaks; the `Py*` re-export wall is now `#[doc(hidden)]` (a grep found no consumer of those paths). Pinned by a prelude smoke test.
- **`ProtoConvert` derive generates streaming app↔protobuf conversions** (`derive/src/proto_convert.rs`, `src/streaming/proto.rs`, `src/streaming/proto_golden_tests.rs`, ARC-006, derive crate 0.47.0): the ENH-041-style derive approach extends to the protobuf side — `ServerMessage`/`ClientMessage` (including the complexity-43 `try_from`) plus the stats structs and `AgentEntry` now derive both conversion directions, routed through explicit `ToWire`/`FromWire` pairs (no blanket narrowing; percent/mouse-button clamps live in `proto::wire`). proto.rs shrank 1,950 → 1,359 lines; a golden test recorded from the old hand-written code proves byte-identical output across all 39 server and 11 client variants and every lossy decode path.
- **CI runs on pull_request** (`.github/workflows/ci.yml`, ARC-004): version check, lint, FFI drift, and the ubuntu/Python 3.14 test + mux jobs (attach suites included) now fire on every PR with superseded-run cancellation; the full OS×Python matrix, Windows/macOS mux, the feature matrix, `web_term/` drift, and xcframework stay on manual dispatch, and PR clippy runs the lint-check feature set (attach clippy joins after the gated render.rs decomposition lands — two pre-existing `type_complexity` errors there would redden every PR).

- **par-mux attach render mode: the side panel's ` menu ` chip opens a command menu** (`src/mux/attach/render.rs`, `src/mux/attach/mod.rs`, `tests/mux_attach.rs`, `docs/MUX.md`): the panel footer's ` menu ` chip now pops a small themed command menu in the context-menu machinery — `keybinds` (opens the keybinds/help panel), `reload config` (the reload chord's client rebind plus the daemon's `reload-config`), and `detach` (ends the client exactly like prefix `d`, exit 0) — with mouse-selectable rows mapped by the raw host column like the other menus. Before, the chip opened the active workspace's menu, which stays reachable by a right press on a workspace row; the chip no longer depends on a loaded workspace roster. ` new ` keeps opening the new-workspace prompt. The keybinds panel's mouse section lists both chips. Pinned by `compose_command_menu_maps_rows_to_actions`, `menu_chip_opens_the_command_menu`, the per-row `command_menu_*` click tests, and end to end by `render_mode_panel_menu_chip_detaches`.
- **par-mux client lifecycle notifications: `%client-attached` / `%client-left`** (`src/tmux_control.rs`, `src/mux/emit.rs`, `src/mux/server.rs`, `src/mux/attach/render.rs`, `src/mux/attach/mod.rs`, `src/python_bindings/types/notification.rs`, `tests/mux_daemon.rs`, `docs/MUX.md`, `docs/API_REFERENCE.md`): the daemon had no client lifecycle signal — tmux's `%client-detached` was parse-only and never emitted, and nothing announced a new client. A control client's registration (its first control command; hook-only connections never register) now broadcasts `%client-attached <id>` to the clients already registered — never to the joining client, so the registration-replay-before-first-reply ordering is unchanged — and every registered connection's end broadcasts `%client-left <id> [$S @W]`, carrying the session and window it was displaying when it had reported a view (`refresh-client -C`). The leave rides the single connection-teardown site, so a clean disconnect, a socket EOF, and an eviction teardown all emit it, queued after the `%layout-change` of any window the departure re-fit (layout-then-lifecycle, dispatch's order). `client` is the daemon's decimal connection id; `%client-detached` stays parse-only. The attach render and passthrough clients mark their status chrome dirty on both. **Rust API:** `TmuxNotification` gains `ClientAttached { client }` and `ClientLeft { client, session_id, window_id }` — exhaustive matches over the (non-`#[non_exhaustive]`) enum need the two arms; Python `TmuxNotification.notification_type` gains `client-attached`/`client-left`. `list-clients` (a client-count query) is not added. Pinned red-to-green by the two-real-client `client_attach_and_drop_broadcast_lifecycle_lines` plus an emit round-trip; the eviction path is covered by construction (it ends in the same teardown), not by a dedicated eviction test.
- **par-mux multi-client sync: smallest-attached-client window sizing + shared-selection notifications** (`src/mux/tree/mod.rs`, `src/mux/tree/layout_ops.rs`, `src/mux/dispatch.rs`, `src/mux/server.rs`, `src/mux/emit.rs`, `src/mux/attach/render.rs`, `tests/mux_daemon.rs`, `docs/MUX.md`): `refresh-client -C` now records the connection's sizing contribution — its reported grid and the window it displays — and a window's extent is the componentwise minimum over the clients currently displaying it. A plain control or hook connection that never reports a size holds no contribution; a constraining client's disconnect (or a view switch) re-fits the window to the remaining viewers' minimum, a window nobody displays keeps its extent until next displayed, and repeated identical reports are idempotent (the re-fit fires only on an extent change, so the reports reseeds and handshakes replay cannot feed back into a resize loop). Embedder/test dispatches without a connection id keep the legacy direct-resize behavior, and a streaming mirror viewer's `Resize` is now a sizing contribution under the same rule (previously latest-report-wins — the last report resized the window outright). Two selection notifications make multi-client sync real: `select-window` on the displayed session now emits `%session-window-changed $N @N` (previously parser-only, never emitted — B never learned A's tab switch), and `select-workspace` adds `%client-session-changed <workspace> $N <name>` when the displayed session moved (the `client` field carries the workspace, par-mux has no client names). The attach renderer parks these in its event handler and performs the follow on the pump with queries and a reseed — it never re-selects, so the broadcast cannot echo into a loop — an external `%window-pane-changed` on the shown window re-points the local focus highlight, and a reseed clears the parked follow state (no stale events). Pinned by five two-real-client daemon tests (minimum sizing stable under repeated reports, disconnect grows the window, tab/workspace switch broadcast, unreported control client never constrains, and the followed window's screen reconstructed in a real `Terminal` from the resync replay) plus five tree-level rule tests and two emit round-trips.
- **par-mux attach render mode round 8: workspaces-only selection follows clicks, bare-name tabs, menu row mapping, raw rename names** (`src/mux/attach/render.rs`, `src/mux/attach/tabs.rs`, `tests/mux_attach.rs`, `docs/MUX.md`): clicking a workspace row now SELECTS it daemon-side (`land_on_workspace` sends `select-workspace` before the landing) — the click used to re-seed the view while the daemon's active-workspace pointer stayed put, leaving the side panel's highlight on the old workspace (the manual-pass report; the keybind path already selected). Tab strips render the bare NAME — the `N:` ordinal prefix is gone, matching the reference rendering (every strip unit/integration pin updated to the narrower tabs). The context menu's click mapping drops an off-by-one: the overlay paints at buffer rows rebased +1 host row by the frame, so panel row r sits at host y0+2+r — the old mapping read every click one row low and fired the neighboring action (pinned by the menu-click tests moved to true geometry; the picker's clicks inherit the same fix). And `rename-window` commits send the RAW name: the daemon grammar takes the rest of the line verbatim, so the wire quoting added in round 6 became literal quotes in tab names (the value-flag commits — `select-pane -T`, `new-window -n`, `rename-workspace`, `new-workspace -n` — keep their quoting; their flags parse it). And the restart CLI test helper drains both piped streams while waiting — a pipe fills at 64 KB and the parent half's stderr announcements once stalled behind an undrained pipe, deadlocking `wait_exited` (the round-8 gate).
- **par-mux daemon: `new-workspace` spawns the workspace's first session** (`src/mux/dispatch.rs`, `tests/mux_daemon.rs`, `tests/mux_restart.rs`): creating a workspace no longer leaves an empty roster row the attach client's view can never reach — `new-workspace` now also spawns the workspace's FIRST session/window (named after the workspace, the default shell) through the same two-phase spawn every session rides, so the side panel's ` new ` chip lands the view on a usable workspace the moment it exists (the manual-pass round-7 report: the created workspace highlighted in the side bar while the demo tabs stayed up and nothing could switch). The wire reply stays the workspace id; `%workspaces-changed` and `%sessions-changed` both ride the create, and the restart restore carries the auto-spawned session (the restart test's exact-id asserts moved to workspace+name pairing). The keybinds panel's categories reordered to `global, workspaces, tabs/windows/sessions, panes, navigation, mouse` (the manual-pass ask).
- **Standalone par-mux release binaries on GitHub Releases** (`.github/workflows/deployment.yml`, `scripts/package_mux_release.py`, `Makefile`, `docs/MUX.md`, `README.md`): the release workflow gains a `build-mux-binaries` job — five `par-mux` daemon targets (Linux x86_64/ARM64 native on `ubuntu-24.04-arm`, macOS ARM64 native and x86_64 cross on `macos-latest`, Windows x86_64) built `--locked --release --no-default-features --features rust-only,mux-bin,attach --bin par-mux` (no Python, no par-term dependency; the `mux` → `serde` closure keeps tree persistence compiled in). Native targets smoke-test `--version`/`--help`; the macOS x86_64 cross build is never executed (Rosetta not assumed). `scripts/package_mux_release.py` (stdlib-only, `make mux-package-check` self-test added to `checkall`) stages the executable + `LICENSE` + a generated install note and archives `par-mux-v<version>-<target>.tar.gz`/`.zip` with normalized mtimes and a preserved 0755 exec bit, plus a per-target sha256 file the `github-release` job merges into `par-mux-v<version>-SHA256SUMS.txt`; archives upload under the `mux-binaries-*` artifact pattern. Install docs (extract → copy into `PATH`; Windows adds a `PATH` directory) live in docs/MUX.md and README.md.
- **par-mux attach render mode round 6: coherent panel-up top row, workspaces-only side panel, context menus, right-aligned Help chip** (`src/mux/attach/render.rs`, `src/mux/attach/tabs.rs`, `src/mux/attach/status.rs`, `src/mux/attach/input.rs`, `src/mux/attach/mod.rs`, `tests/mux_attach.rs`, `docs/MUX.md`): the round-5 build shipped a real click defect — `route_mouse` rebased the host column by the side panel's width BEFORE the tab-strip branch, so with the panel up every top-row click mapped one panel width left and the ` + ` button was unclickable. Round 6 makes the geometry coherent instead: with the panel up the TAB STRIP paints the panel's ` workspaces ` title accent in the panel's columns (shared row 0, the design mock's header), lays the tabs out from them onward with ` + ` still pinned at the strip's right edge, and the hit-test takes the RAW host column through that offset (lead columns hit nothing) — pinned red-to-green by `render_mode_plus_click_works_with_the_panel_up` and at the unit level by `lead_segment_offsets_the_tabs_and_keeps_the_plus_pinned`. The side panel lists WORKSPACES ONLY (the nested `win:` rows and their dispatch arm are gone; `render_mode_workspace_click_reseeds_with_the_panel` moved its click up a row with the header) and drops its section header rows — the title moved to the strip's lead segment — while a new footer row pins ` new ` (the new-workspace prompt, default the next non-conflicting workspace index via `next_workspace_name`, commit sending `new-workspace -n <quoted name>` then `select-workspace` + landing the view on the fresh workspace) and ` menu ` (the ACTIVE workspace's menu) to the panel's bottom-left/bottom-right, each chip its own clickable span and id (`panel:new`/`panel:menu`). Context menus arrive: a RIGHT press on a tab opens that window's menu and a right press on a workspace row (or the ` menu ` chip) opens the workspace menu — both a small modal in the picker's overlay machinery (`rename` / `close` / `add tab`|`new`, esc/q closes, a click on an action row dispatches, elsewhere consumes; the pure row→action mapping pinned by `compose_menu_panel_maps_rows_to_actions`, the right-press decode by `right_press_decodes_and_rejects_its_lookalikes`). Rename targets carry their id (`PromptTarget::Window(+id)`/`PromptTarget::Workspace(+id)`), so renaming from a menu hits the CLICKED window, not the shown one, and every prompt commit now quotes its name through `wire_quote` — including the pre-existing `select-pane -T` and `rename-window` paths (closes the unquoted-names follow-up card). Kills recover: closing the SHOWN window lands the view on its session's surviving daemon-active (or first) window, closing the shown workspace lands on the first surviving workspace, and none remaining ends the view cleanly through the refresh's SessionGone. And the status bar's ` C-b? Help ` chip RIGHT-ALIGNS at the row's right edge — the reservation's width math unchanged, the paint now clips the content at the chip's left column so a full line can never push the chip off the row (pinned by `paint_right_aligns_the_chip_and_clips_content_at_it`).

### Security
- **Streaming server warns on weak Basic Auth credentials** (`src/streaming/config.rs`, `src/streaming/auth_hash.rs`, SEC-201/SEC-206): configuring a cleartext password or a legacy `{SHA}` / `$1$` / `$apr1$` htpasswd hash now logs a `warn!` naming the format and pointing at `htpasswd -nB`. The message never contains the secret; verification is unchanged.
- **Streaming server warns when `--allow-api-key-in-query` is active** (`src/streaming/server.rs`, SEC-202): startup logs a `warn!` when query-string API keys are accepted, since they land in proxy logs and browser history.
- **`--download-frontend` verifies the archive's SHA-256** (`src/bin/streaming_server/frontend_download.rs`, `.github/workflows/deployment.yml`, SEC-203): releases now ship a `par-term-web-frontend-v<version>.tar.gz.sha256` sidecar, and the download checks it before the web root is touched. A mismatch or unusable sidecar aborts. Releases without a sidecar download with a warning.
- **Web frontend `dev`/`start` scripts bind to `127.0.0.1`** (`web-terminal-frontend/package.json`, SEC-205): LAN testing now needs an explicit `-H 0.0.0.0`. `.gitignore` also covers `.env`, `.env.local`, and `.env*.local` (SEC-204).

### Fixed
- **`ZoneScrolledOut` events now reach observers and filtered polls** (`src/terminal/mod.rs`): the events were created inside `poll_events()` from the grids' evicted-zone buffers and drained in the same call, so `TerminalObserver::on_zone_event` never saw them and `poll_subscribed_events()` with a filter set never returned them. Evicted zones are now pushed onto the event queue at the end of `process()`/`process_deferred()`, before observer dispatch, and before a filtered `poll_subscribed_events()`. `Terminal::apply_action()`/`apply_actions()` (`src/terminal/action.rs`), which drive the parser without `process()`, now run the same tail, so observers receive the events they queue (zone evictions included) before the call returns. Previously those events waited for the next `process()`. `Terminal::resize()` (and `ptec_terminal_resize`) runs the same tail for zones a narrowing reflow pushes out of scrollback; `PtySession::resize()`/`resize_with_pixels()` deliver them after dropping the terminal write guard, as the PTY reader thread does. `poll_events()` still flushes too, for zones evicted by a `Grid` mutated directly. Pinned by `test_zone_scrolled_out_reaches_observers_via_process`, `..._via_process_deferred`, `..._via_apply_actions`, `..._via_apply_action`, `..._via_resize`, `test_resize_eviction_reaches_observers_without_write_lock`, and `test_zone_scrolled_out_reaches_filtered_poll`.
- **par-mux `paste-buffer -t <missing pane>` with an empty buffer now names the pane** (`src/mux/dispatch.rs`): the empty-buffer check ran before the pane lookup, so a typo'd target answered `no buffers` and hid the real mistake. The pane is checked first. Pinned by `paste_buffer_reports_the_bad_target_before_the_empty_buffer`.
- **par-mux attach render mode: switching to a workspace with fewer tabs left the old tabs on the strip** (`src/mux/attach/tabs.rs`): `TabStrip::invalidate()` reset its diff baseline to a BLANK row, and the diff emits only cells that differ from the baseline, so the new strip's blank cells were never written. The reseed, resize, and sidebar-toggle paths invalidate without clearing host row 0, so the previous workspace's extra tabs stayed on screen wherever the new strip was blank. The invalidated baseline is now filled with a sentinel glyph no paint writes, so the next diff repaints every column, blanks included. `StatusRow::invalidate` does not share the flaw: its padding is styled REVERSED, so every column already differs from a blank baseline. Pinned red-to-green by `invalidate_repaints_every_column_including_blanks`.
- **par-mux attach render mode: every client's ` Z ` zoom cue follows the daemon's per-window zoom truth** (`src/mux/attach/render.rs`, `tests/mux_daemon.rs`, `docs/MUX.md`): the status row's ` Z ` cue was per-client state flipped only by the client's own zoom chord, so a second attached client never showed another client's `resize-pane -Z` (and a select-away unzoom left a stale cue on the zooming client until its next chord). The daemon already broadcasts zoom truth per window as the `Z` raw flag of `%layout-change` from every site that changes it (`resize-pane -Z` both ways, `select-pane` away, every layout mutation) — no new notification. The render client now sets the cue from that flag for the shown window in its event handler (outside the geometry guard, so a one-pane window's same-rect zoom still flips it), and in the seed, reseed, and host-resize paths that read the triple directly; another window's zoom never touches it, the chord still flips it locally for instant feedback, and passthrough keeps chrome-dirtying on `%layout-change`. Pinned red-to-green by `layout_change_flags_drive_the_zoom_cue_for_the_shown_window_only`, and over the wire by the two-client `a_zoom_and_its_select_away_unzoom_reach_the_other_client`.
- **par-mux attach render mode round 7: the context menu's clicks were one panel width left of its items** (`src/mux/attach/render.rs`, `tests/mux_attach.rs`): the menu modal centers over the HOST width (the pane layout already carries the strip offset), but its mouse guard mapped clicks through the panel's strip rebase — with the panel up every action row was unclickable (the manual-pass report; the same defect class as round 5's unclickable ` + `). The guard takes the raw host column like the picker's (red-to-green pinned by `panel_up_menu_click_maps_raw_host_columns` and the panel-up end-to-end tab-menu test), and the strip's active-tab block is pinned never to bleed into a neighboring tab's columns with or without the lead segment (`active_block_never_bleeds_into_other_tabs`).

### Added
- **par-mux attach render mode round 5: refit strip repaint, herdr default, status-bar chrome, tab `+`/new-tab prompt, help scrollbar** (`src/mux/attach/render.rs`, `src/mux/attach/status.rs`, `src/mux/attach/tabs.rs`, `src/mux/attach/mod.rs`, `src/mux/config.rs`, `tests/mux_attach.rs`, `docs/MUX.md`): the refit's full repaint (`ESC[2J`) cleared the tab-strip and status rows too, but both rows flush diff-based and nothing invalidated them on the sidebar-toggle refit path — part of the status bar (and the whole tab strip) stayed wiped after bringing the panel up (pinned by `render_mode_sidebar_toggle_repaints_the_strip_rows`, red-proofed against the fix). `herdr` (per-pane boxes) is now the default border style — `[client] border-lines` defaults to `herdr`, so the seed paints per-pane rounded boxes and the `pane-borders` paint mode follows the style's own default (pinned by the rewritten `render_mode_border_style_survives_split`: seed `╭`, a full cycle returns, and the chord split keeps them). The status bar ends with a ` C-b? Help ` chip — the live help keybind the way the keybinds panel spells it, its width reserved before compose so it survives truncation (unit-pinned by `help_chip_spells_the_live_keybind`); while hidden the bottom row is never flushed at all, so the grown pane bottom row can never be erased (pinned by `hidden_status_bar_flushes_nothing`). Prefix `S` (`[client] status-bar`) toggles the bottom bar — hidden by default-shown, the pane grid grows the row, and the bar's vanishing is its own feedback (a flash would paint onto the hidden row; pinned by `render_mode_status_bar_toggle_hides_and_restores_the_row` via the cursor's host-row shift both ways). The tab strip's ` + ` button owns a reserved three-column slot at the strip's right edge (the tab area truncates beside it, so the button never disappears on crowded strips): a click opens herdr's new-tab prompt (` new tab ` title, `enter save · ^c clear · esc cancel` footer, `^C` clearing the input) seeded with the next non-conflicting window index (bumped past any window NAME claiming the number; unit-pinned by `next_window_name_bumps_past_conflicts`), Enter sending `new-window -t <session> -n <name>` and landing the view on the fresh window (end to end: `render_mode_plus_click_prompts_and_creates_a_window`). And the keybinds modal paints a `┃` scroll thumb on the ring's right column when the content overflows (positioned from the scroll state, pinned by `help_overlay_paints_a_scrollbar_thumb_when_it_overflows`), while the idle help and picker panels drop the `press / to filter` placeholder row — the footers already advertise `search /` and `filter /`; the filter line still appears the instant `/` opens it (compose and renderer tests updated).

### Added
- **par-mux attach render mode: sidebar re-seed and border-cycle defects from the manual pass** (`src/mux/attach/render.rs`, `tests/mux_attach.rs`): the border-cycle chord flips the session-level `pane-borders` flag now, not just the renderer's — the chord changed the renderer's copy only, so the split chord's re-seed restored the session flag and every cycled-to herdr box set reverted to shared dividers on the first split (pinned by `render_mode_border_style_survives_split`, which splits through the chord). Re-seeds rebuild the renderer at the HOST grid and report the fresh `window_size` — the re-seed used to construct the renderer at the REPORTED width (grid less the side panel), ratcheting the view one panel-width narrower on every re-seed while the panel was up, each report then shrinking again (the click-a-workspace-row-shrinks-the-panes report; pinned by the two-click geometry asserts in `render_mode_workspace_click_reseeds_with_the_panel`). And the panel's nested window rows carry a child indent so a window row can no longer be clicked as if it were a second workspace row.
- **par-mux attach render mode: three sidebar/label/border defects from the manual pass** (`src/mux/attach/render.rs`, `src/mux/attach/status.rs`, `tests/mux_attach.rs`): pane content no longer paints the side panel's width twice — the content inset (`content_view`) silently carried the strip offset because `display_rect` already applied it, so with the strip up every pane's content shifted right twice (clipped at the frame edge, dead margin between the divider and the pushed content) while dividers and borders moved once (pinned at the renderer level by `sidebar_offset_paints_content_and_dividers_together`, end to end at 41→51→41 columns across the toggle by `render_mode_sidebar_toggle_refits_pane_geometry`). Every re-seed path now re-applies the session's chosen border-glyph set and the panel width/rows to the fresh renderer — splitting no longer reverts a cycled-to herdr border set to plain Unicode singles, and a panel-row click's re-seed no longer blanks the panel (pinned by `render_mode_border_style_survives_split` and `render_mode_workspace_click_reseeds_with_the_panel`). And in the strip-up top row, the solid shaded block moved from the active window's tab to the active workspace's label (the active tab is the bold run instead) — with both shaded, the line read as selecting a different workspace than the panel highlighted (pinned by the extended `compose_sidebar_mode_leads_with_the_active_workspace`).
- **par-mux side panel refinements from the manual pass** (`src/mux/attach/render.rs`, `src/mux/attach/status.rs`, `src/mux/attach/mod.rs`): the sidebar nests each workspace's sessions' windows dim beneath its row (herdr's tabs-under-workspace look) with prefixed row ids — a workspace-row click lands on the workspace, a window-row click selects that window — and the ACTIVE workspace paints as herdr's full-width inverted block (not just a brighter glyph run); with the strip up, the top status row switches to herdr's compact form, leading with the active workspace's label before the tabs instead of the all-workspaces/sessions rosters the strip now carries. Also fixes a leftover debug probe that printed raw `list-workspaces` replies into the focused pane on every sidebar refresh.
- **par-mux attach render mode: toggleable workspace side panel** (`src/mux/attach/render.rs`, `src/mux/attach/mod.rs`, `src/mux/config.rs`, `tests/mux_attach.rs`): prefix `s` (`[client] sidebar`, width `[client] sidebar-width`, default 20 clamped 6–60) toggles herdr's left strip — themed-bg fill, dim right-edge divider, sections composed top-down by the pure `compose_sidebar` (the queried workspace roster first: accent header, `▸`-marked active row, click-to-land through the shared select+resync; built to take more sections). While up, the client reports the reduced grid (`window_size` minus the strip) so the daemon re-divides around it, every pane/border/divider/cursor/mouse coordinate maps through the strip offset (the paint clamp bounds the pre-broadcast frame), the roster re-queries on open and with every throttled status refresh, and toggling off reports the full width back. Pinned by `render_mode_sidebar_toggle_minimal` (toggle on: flash + strip section + reduced-division re-seed; toggle off: restore) and `compose_sidebar_renders_sections_and_marks_active`.
- **par-mux attach render mode: workspace picker, scroll-position indicator, default-on pane labels, modal footer band, and shift-arrow feedback** (`src/mux/attach/render.rs`, `src/mux/attach/mod.rs`, `src/mux/config.rs`, `tests/mux_attach.rs`): prefix `g` (`[client] workspace-picker`) opens the session/window picker's workspace sibling — the daemon's workspace roster in the same themed modal (filter, cursor, clicks, dark-grey footer band), Enter landing through the same select-then-resync; a scrolled pane shows a `▐` position marker in its right edge even without `scrollbar-gutter` (the divider yields the column while it shows); `show-label-in-border` now defaults ON — pane titles show in their borders in the per-pane-box modes, prefix `l` still toggles; the help/picker/prompt modals' controls footers paint on a dark-grey band; and the shift+arrow swap flashes `swapped …` (or `no pane in that direction` at an edge) and matches the shift bit loosely, so a terminal co-reporting modifiers still swaps (the manual-pass "prefix+S arrows does not work" report). Pinned by the extended `render_mode_zoom_rename_border_chords` (workspace picker open/dismiss, label toggle polarity) and the compose/overlay units.
- **par-mux attach render mode: herdr border style, pane-label toggle, directional swap, and a herdr-shaded tab strip** (`src/mux/attach/render.rs`, `src/mux/attach/status.rs`, `src/mux/attach/mod.rs`, `src/mux/config.rs`, `tests/mux_attach.rs`): the border cycle gains a fifth style, `herdr` — every pane draws its own complete rounded box (the style owns the paint mode; an explicit `pane-borders` still overrides for any glyph set), selectable live with prefix `B` or `[client] border-lines = "herdr"`; prefix `l` toggles each pane's title embedded in its border (`[client] label-toggle`, default `l`; labels paint in the per-pane-box modes); Shift+arrows swap the focused pane with the pane in that direction (`swap-pane`, focus follows the content, at an edge nothing moves); and the status row's workspace/window tabs render herdr-style — the active tab a solid shaded block, the rest dim with `|` separators (the `*` marker and `{window}:` prefixes are gone; the tabs are names). Pinned by the extended `render_mode_zoom_rename_border_chords`, `compose_shades_the_active_workspace_only`, and `compose_bolds_the_current_session_and_shades_the_active_window`.
- **par-mux `resize-pane` resizes on both axes** (`src/mux/tree/layout_ops.rs`, `src/mux/tree/tests.rs`): the relative form now walks to the nearest same-axis ancestor split at any depth (tmux semantics — the extent walk `set_leaf_extent` already had), so a pane nested under a cross-orientation split (say a stacked pair inside a side-by-side root) resizes with `-U`/`-D` AND `-L`/`-R`; before, only the pane's own bordering split answered and the other axis errored `PaneNotResizable`. Wire semantics unchanged (`-R`/`-D` grow the pane, `-L`/`-U` shrink), lone panes and single-axis windows still error. Pinned by `resize_pane_reaches_a_cross_orientation_ancestor`.
- **par-mux attach render mode: zoom, rename, and border-style chords** (`src/mux/attach/render.rs`, `src/mux/attach/mod.rs`, `src/mux/config.rs`, `tests/mux_attach.rs`): `prefix z` rides the daemon's existing `resize-pane -Z` — the focused pane's PTY takes the full window grid, the `%layout-change` re-seed repaints it, select-away unzooms (all the daemon's zoom rules apply), and a bold ` Z ` cue marks the status row; `,` opens a modal rename prompt (seeded with the current name; Enter commits as `rename-window`, Escape cancels); `$` is the pane-title variant (`select-pane -T`); `B` cycles the divider/border glyphs unicode → double → heavy → ascii, live; prefix+arrow keys select the nearest pane in the arrow's direction (while zoomed they aim through the cached tree geometry, the daemon unzooms on the select). All four chords rebind via `[client] zoom` / `rename-window` / `rename-pane` / `border-cycle`, live on reload; the startup glyph set is `[client] border-lines` (`unicode|double|heavy|ascii`, default `unicode`). Passthrough consumes the four chords unbound (render-only). Pinned by `render_mode_zoom_rename_border_chords` (`tests/mux_attach.rs`), `pane_in_direction_picks_the_nearest_pane_in_the_direction` and the help-rows update (`src/mux/attach/render.rs`, `src/mux/attach/mod.rs`).
- **par-mux split-window/new-window tmux target parity** (`src/mux/ids.rs`, `src/mux/command.rs`, `src/mux/dispatch.rs`, `src/mux/tree/`): `split-window -t` accepts `$N`/`@N`/name in addition to `%N` (window → its active pane, session → its active window's active pane); `new-window -t @N`/`%N` inserts the new window immediately after that window (active index follows the shift). Pinned by `tests/mux_targets.rs` and tree tests. Landed from a parallel worktree agent's branch, squash-merged.
- **par-mux gate: the attach feature joins every mux gate** (`Makefile`, `.github/workflows/ci.yml`): the mux suite and CI clippy never enabled the `attach` feature, so the attach client's lib tests and the `mux_attach` PTY suite compiled in NO gate — the horizontal-divider regression (below) shipped through three green gates that way. `attach` now rides the mux suite line and the clippy line (CI and local).
- **par-mux `[daemon] exit-empty` toggle** (`src/mux/config.rs`, `src/mux/server.rs`, `docs/MUX.md`): the exit-when-empty shutdown is now configurable — `exit-empty = false` holds a persisting daemon past its grace however long it sits empty; the default `true` is the shipped behavior. Second live daemon setting: `reload-config` (or the `C-b C-r` chord) applies a changed value without a restart. gen-config emits the row. Pinned by `exit_empty_false_keeps_an_empty_persisting_server_alive` (`src/mux/server.rs`), `reload_applies_exit_empty_live` and `exit_empty_defaults_to_on` (`src/mux/config.rs`).
- **par-mux `attach` render mode: herdr-parity display options — `pane-gaps`, `scrollbar-gutter`, `drag-cursor-shape`** (`src/mux/attach/render.rs`, `src/mux/config.rs`): three independent `[client]` options, ALL DEFAULT OFF (the current look renders byte-identical, pinned by test). `pane-gaps` (cells, default 0) insets each pane's rect per side so theme-bg gap bands separate the panes (content, borders, labels, and cursor mapping follow; the boundary dividers keep their geometry); `scrollbar-gutter` reserves a right-edge gutter column per pane rect (content narrows by one; a minimal scroll-position indicator shows while the pane's client scrollback view is scrolled, and the boundary divider yields the gutter column); `drag-cursor-shape` shapes the host cursor to the resize shape (best-effort DECSCUSR steady block) while a divider drag is live and restores on drag end. gen-config emits all three; the reload chord rebinds them live.
- **par-mux `attach` render mode: session/window picker modal on prefix `w`** (`src/mux/attach/mod.rs`, `src/mux/attach/render.rs`, `src/mux/config.rs`): `[client] picker` (default `w`) opens a herdr-style picker over the frame reusing the help panel's modal chrome (theme bg, accent ring, `esc close` badge, footer controls line) — sessions with their windows nested (`>` marks the current session/window, `*` the session's active window), a `/` filter-as-you-type box, arrows/`j`/`k` selection, Enter or click select (select-window + resync; a session header lands on its active window), esc/`q` dismiss; the help panel lists the chord at its effective binding, gen-config emits the key, and the reload chord rebinds it live. The `W`/`C-w` workspace chords and `?` are unchanged.
- **High-risk adversarial test coverage for the new mux surfaces** (test-only except where noted; `fuzz/corpus/mux_parse_command/`, `src/mux/command.rs` (test), `src/mux/attach/input.rs` tests, `src/mux/discovery.rs` tests, `src/mux/config.rs` tests, `tests/test_notification_workspace.py` new): ten fuzz corpus seeds for the workspace-era wire commands (`new-workspace`, `list-workspaces`, `select-workspace`/`rename-workspace`/`kill-workspace` `-t +N`, `list-sessions -t +N`, `reload-config`, `list-windows -t $N`, `list-panes -t @N`) plus a corpus regression test that drives every seed through `parse_command`/`parse_line` where cargo-fuzz is not run; an adversarial suite for the render client's `InputParser` (byte-at-a-time decoding, every byte-boundary split of a paste marker, hostile paste bodies, bursts interleaving paste/chord/plain runs, and the unterminated-paste cap); discovery-registry corruption coverage (garbage/truncated/wrong-typed/empty/1MB registry files are skipped without panic, a hostile socket entry probes dead and is pruned); hostile config-file coverage (wrong types, unknown keys, a 1MB value, invalid UTF-8, duplicate keys, a directory-shaped path — classified errors, never panics, plus a gen-config round-trip); and a Python binding test pinning `%workspaces-changed` through `drain_tmux_notifications`.
- **par-mux `attach`: workspaces surface — status segment, `W`/`C-w` switching chords, and `%workspaces-changed` staleness** (`src/mux/attach/status.rs`, `src/mux/attach/mod.rs`, `src/mux/attach/render.rs`, `src/mux/config.rs`)
- **par-mux `[daemon] remain-on-exit` toggle, defaulted to auto-remove** (`src/mux/config.rs`, `src/mux/server.rs`, `src/mux/persist.rs`)
- **par-mux `attach --mode render`: clickable tab strip across the top row (par-term style)** (`src/mux/attach/tabs.rs` new, `src/mux/attach/mod.rs`, `src/mux/attach/render.rs`, `src/mux/attach/status.rs`, `tests/mux_attach.rs`, `docs/MUX.md`; feature `attach`): the render-mode frame gains a one-row TAB STRIP at the host's top row — one tab per window of the shown session (`N:name`, window order), the active tab in the theme accent (indexed 14, bold), the inactive tabs dim. The strip reads the SAME queried state as the status bar (the `list-windows -t` half of the throttled re-query), so a window add/close/rename (`%sessions-changed` family) refreshes it with the status row, and it follows the same redraw discipline (invalidate on seed/resize/re-seed, diff-based flush so churn repaints only changed cells). Truncation keeps the active tab always visible (the par-term tab-bar rule, `/Users/probello/Repos/par-term/src/tab_bar_ui/tab_painter.rs`): full names when they fit; otherwise every name truncated to the widest shared budget with an ellipsis; otherwise a contiguous run of tabs containing the active one, `…` markers at each hidden edge. A click on a tab hits the painted layout (paint and hit-testing share one `layout_tabs` function, so what a click highlights is what painted) and switches to that window through the existing select+resync contract (`select-window -t` + full re-seed) — a strip click never focuses or forwards into a pane, drags never start on the strip row, and an in-flight divider drag continues when the pointer wanders onto the strip. The content height arithmetic accounts for the strip everywhere the status row already did: the renderer is built at rows−2 (strip + status), the pump's resize re-fit reports `refresh-client -C cols x (rows−2)`, the pane diff and cursor placement rebase one host row down, the strip flushes at host row 0, and the help modal centers within the shrunk content area. Frame painting is now bounds-safe against a stale layout racing a host shrink (a rect past the buffer edge clips instead of panicking). Pinned by the `tabs::` unit suite (10 tests: placement, shared-budget truncation, active-visible runs, hit-testing, accent/dim painting, diff discipline) and, in `src/mux/attach/render.rs`, `session_height_carves_the_strip_and_status_out`, `frame_flushes_the_strip_at_row_zero_and_rebases_the_panes`, and `strip_click_switches_windows_without_forwarding` (wire: `select-window` + the 80x23 content size report, no pane traffic); PTY-level by `render_mode_tab_click_switches_the_active_window` (`tests/mux_attach.rs`, the daemon's active-window marker moves). The mouse-forward and host-resize pins updated to the strip-shifted coordinates (`0;10;4M` pane-relative row, 100x28 content grid). Commit `<this>`.
- **par-mux `attach` render mode: per-pane border boxes and border labels (default off)** (`src/mux/config.rs`, `src/mux/attach/render.rs`, `docs/MUX.md`; feature `attach`, drawing on herdr's pane chrome in `~/Repos/herdr/src/ui/panes.rs`): `[client] pane-borders = true` replaces the shared-divider look with a complete box-drawing ring per pane — the focused pane's border bright-cyan bold, the rest dim, content inset by the border cells (the cursor placement maps through the same inset) — and `[client] show-label-in-border = true` embeds each pane's user title in its top edge, space-padded and truncated to fit (label cells are not drag handles; the plain segments stay draggable). Both default off, live-rebindable on the reload chord, rendered by `gen-config`. Pinned by `pane_borders_option_replaces_dividers_with_full_boxes` and `label_in_border_embeds_title_and_blocks_drag_on_label_cells` (`src/mux/attach/render.rs`). Commit `<this>`.
- **par-mux `attach` render mode round 3: probe, re-seed, cursor, drag, and help-panel fixes** (`src/mux/attach/conn.rs`, `src/mux/attach/input.rs`, `src/mux/attach/render.rs`, `src/mux/attach/mod.rs`, `src/mux/config.rs`, `docs/MUX.md`; feature `attach`): (1) **OSC 11 probe**: the black fallback on a failed probe is GONE — a failed probe leaves the frame fill at the terminal default (no painted color), so it can never mismatch the theme; default-to-black only renders when the probe SUCCEEDED with black. The probe's own parser had the field bug: 4-hex-digit components (Ghostty's 16-bit `rgb:1e1e/…` replies) shift-overflowed `1u16 << 16` and produced garbage/black — fixed in u32 and pinned by `osc_color_report_parses_and_yields_gracefully`. The race audit: the probe and the pump's stdin reader never overlap (the reader is born after the probe returns); a reply that lands after the 150 ms deadline is swallowed whole by the input parser (`late_osc11_reply_is_dropped_not_forwarded`) instead of fragmenting into the pane. (2) **Re-seed bg loss**: `prefix n`/`p` (and resize re-fit) rebuilt the renderer, silently dropping the probed background back to black — the session now keeps the resolved bg and re-applies it across every reconstruction (pinned by the fill tests). (3) **Cursor off-by-one**: `replay_all_panes` appended an extra `\n` to the re-joined replay body, driving the freshly positioned cursor one row below the shell prompt — the join is now exact (pinned by `focused_cursor_maps_through_the_rect_and_the_border_inset`). (4) **Divider drag starvation**: the second motion event destroyed the active drag (`take()` on an Active state), so the resize fired once at most while the highlight stayed up — promotion now only applies to a PENDING press (pinned by `drag_survives_active_motion_and_resizes_per_cell`). (5) **Help panel**: the modal paints the resolved theme bg on every cell (no more default/light cells), a rounded box-drawing ring in the accent with the `keybinds` title and `esc close` badge embedded in the top border, the filter line is ALWAYS visible (placeholder when inactive, ` /query▌` cursor the moment `/` is pressed), and with the panel open wheels scroll the PANEL instead of falling through to the pane (pinned by `help_modal_paints_theme_bg_accent_ring_and_title`, `compose_help_panel_filters_scrolls_and_chromes`, and `help_open_consumes_wheel_events`). Commit `<this>`.
- **par-mux `attach`: pane resize affordances — prefix+arrow resize chords and divider mouse-drag** (`src/mux/config.rs`, `src/mux/attach/mod.rs`, `src/mux/attach/render.rs`, `src/mux/attach/conn.rs`, `Cargo.toml`, `docs/MUX.md`; feature `attach`): splits can now be resized from a live attach, on top of the daemon wire's existing `resize-pane` forms. **Resize chords** (both modes): prefix `R` (settable as `[client] resize`, tmux spelling or literal through the shared prefix grammar) enters a sticky resize mode — arrows send the wire's relative `resize-pane -t <focused> -L|-R|-U|-D <step>` at the configured step (`[client] resize-step`, default 1, floored at 1) until Enter/Escape/`q` exits; any other key leaves the mode and is reprocessed by the normal router (in passthrough it forwards, chords still need the prefix; in render mode it is consumed). The mode entry flashes a cue on the status row/flash path; the step rebinds live on the reload chord. **Divider mouse-drag** (render mode): a press within one cell of a divider starts a pending drag — it never focuses or forwards (a divider press is not a click-through), motion promotes it and maps the pointer's cell delta along the divider's axis to the same relative `resize-pane` aimed at the boundary's left/top pane, applied one cell per unapplied delta at the frame cadence (the `%layout-change` the resize queues re-seeds the window through the existing pending-layout path), and the dragged divider renders reversed while the drag lasts; a release without motion falls through as a click (the pane under the pointer still focuses, `select-pane` rides the wire, and the release forwards when the pane owns the mouse). **Swap chords:** prefix `{`/`}` (settable as `[client] swap-prev`/`swap-next`, tmux's spellings) send `swap-pane -s <focused> -t <neighbor>` from the layout-order roster (`swap-pane -s %0 -t %1` for a two-pane window's prev wrap); render mode lets the swap's `%layout-change` re-seed the window. **Help panel:** prefix `?` (settable as `[client] help`) opens the bindings modal — a bordered panel titled `keybinds` with an `esc close` badge and a footer controls line (`search / · scroll j/k/arrows/pgup/pgdn · close esc/enter`); entries group into category headers (`global`, `panes`, `windows/sessions`, **`navigation`**, `mouse`) with each row showing the chord at its EFFECTIVE binding (a remapped chord shows its remapped key), `/` opens a filter-as-you-type box (Backspace edits, Enter commits), j/k/arrows/PgUp/PgDn scroll the content window, esc/Enter/q close and the prior frame repaints; keys never reach the pane while it is up. Passthrough prints the same category rows as plain text. **Host background probe:** the renderer's frame fill now probes the host with OSC 11 (`ESC ] 11 ; ? ST`, poll(2)-based, 150 ms deadline, unix-only; black stays the fallback) right after raw mode comes up and before the pump's stdin reader starts, so a dark-grey host theme no longer paints black frames, and a corrected `set-client-colors` rides the probe. **Focus indicator side-naming** (manual-pass item 2): each divider boundary names its side — bright cyan when the focused pane is the boundary's left/top pane, bright magenta when it is the right/bottom pane — so a focus switch flips the shared two-pane divider's color visibly (a single accent read identically from either side). All new chords and the step are live-rebindable on the reload chord. Pinned by `resize_chord_arrows_send_resize_pane_and_q_exits`, `resize_mode_cancelling_key_reprocesses_normally`, `resize_chord_and_step_follow_the_config`, `swap_prev_chord_sends_swap_pane`, `help_rows_carry_effective_bindings_and_categories`, and `compose_help_panel_filters_scrolls_and_chromes` (`src/mux/attach/mod.rs`); `drag_on_divider_resizes_without_clicking_through`, `click_near_divider_without_drag_still_focuses`, `focus_flip_changes_the_shared_dividers_color`, `render_resize_chord_sends_for_the_focused_pane`, and `help_chord_opens_filters_and_dismissal_restores_the_frame` (`src/mux/attach/render.rs`); and `resize_chord_and_step_parse_with_the_step_floored` (`src/mux/config.rs`). The btop-exit stale-cell artifact (manual-pass item 4) was NOT reproduced headlessly in this round; the repro shape is an alt-screen session with styled cells fed to a pane emulator, exit via `1049l`, re-seed, and check the renderer diff for stale bg — queued for the next pass. Commit `<this>`.

### Added
- **par-mux `attach` render mode: tmux-convention divider halves and configurable border colors** (`src/mux/attach/render.rs`, `src/mux/config.rs`, `docs/MUX.md`): a shared divider now highlights only the HALF nearer the active pane — a side-by-side split's divider highlights its top half while the left pane is focused and its bottom half while the right one is; horizontal dividers mirror it (the whole-divider cyan/magenta side-naming is gone). The highlight and the unfocused look are configurable via `[client] border-active-color` / `border-color` (`#rrggbb` hex, defaults bright-cyan-accent / dim), applied at attach and live on the reload chord, emitted by gen-config. Pinned by the rewritten `focus_flip_changes_the_shared_dividers_color` and `focus_highlights_adjacent_dividers` (`src/mux/attach/render.rs`) and the config round-trip.
- **par-mux `attach`: render-mode resize arrows follow the divider, and the modal hides the cursor** (`src/mux/attach/render.rs`): resize-mode arrows move the SHARED divider in the pressed direction from either side — a focused pane on the far side of its boundary inverts the wire direction (growing the right pane used to move the divider left; the manual-pass report) — and a modal overlay (help, picker) now hides the host cursor instead of drawing the pane's cursor through the panel, restoring placement on dismissal. Pinned by the extended `render_resize_chord_sends_for_the_focused_pane` and `modal_overlay_hides_the_pane_cursor` (`src/mux/attach/render.rs`).
- **par-mux `attach` passthrough: the status bar redraws itself** (`src/mux/attach/mod.rs`): the bar was drawn once per state change, so a full-screen pane app that wiped the reserved row (or reset the host's scroll margins) left it gone for the rest of the session (the manual-pass htop report). The pump now redraws it every ~0.5 s when nothing else did — one small absolute-CUP run, self-healing under any TUI.

### Changed
- **BEHAVIOR CHANGE: render mode is the default `par-mux attach` pipeline; an unknown `--mode` flag value now fails loudly** (`src/mux/attach/mod.rs`, `src/bin/par_mux/main.rs`, `src/mux/config.rs`, `docs/MUX.md`): attach without `--mode` opens the pane renderer (passthrough is the opt-out, `--mode passthrough`, and stays what the test suite pins); the config-file `mode` tier resolves the same default. A typo'd flag value (`--mode rendered`) used to fall back to passthrough silently — it now exits 2 naming the valid modes; an unknown FILE value warns on stderr and uses the default. `AttachOptions.mode` is `Option<AttachMode>` so the flag tier is distinguishable from the default. Pinned by `attach_mode_flag_parses_and_validates` (`src/bin/par_mux/main.rs`) and the flipped `AttachMode::default`.
- **BEHAVIOR CHANGE: the passthrough attach client no longer uses the host alternate screen** (`src/mux/attach/mod.rs`, `tests/mux_attach.rs`, `docs/MUX.md`): the client entered the host alt screen once at setup, so a pane app's own `1049h`/`1049l` pair (htop, vim) became the SECOND entry — its leave popped the host back to its main screen. The app-exit screen replaced the pane (the manual-pass report: after exiting htop the pre-attach terminal content showed), every later client draw (the status bar) painted the host main and stayed after detach, and the bar under the app was already lost. The client now clears the screen at attach (`ESC[2J ESC[H`), re-clears on every pane-show replay (switches never mix panes), and clears again on detach; pane apps own the alt screen naturally, their rmcup returns to the pane's screen, and the status row's DECSTBM reserve keeps it safe from app scrolling. tmux control mode never enters the host alt either. Pinned by the rewritten `detach_restores_the_terminal_region` (clear at setup and detach, NO `1049h` ever).
- **BEHAVIOR CHANGE: attached clients no longer hold an empty par-mux daemon** (`src/mux/server.rs`, `docs/MUX.md`): exit-when-empty previously required zero CLIENTS and an empty tree, so a daemon whose last session died under an attached client lingered forever — the client sat on a frozen, dead tree with no `%exit` (the manual-pass report: kill the last pane, the daemon never exits). The empty check is now the tree alone (zero sessions, or only dead panes); the last session dying under a client ends the server after the 5 s grace exactly as tmux's `exit-empty` does, and the client receives the broadcast `%exit` and detaches. `[daemon] exit-empty = false` (live via `reload-config`) still holds the daemon indefinitely, client or not. Pinned by `a_connected_client_receives_exit_when_the_tree_empties` and `exit_empty_false_keeps_an_empty_persisting_server_alive` (now with an attached client) in `src/mux/server.rs`.
- **BEHAVIOR CHANGE: a par-mux daemon pane whose child exits is now AUTO-REMOVED by default** (`[daemon] remain-on-exit`, default flipped from the always-on hold to off; `src/mux/config.rs`, `src/mux/server.rs`, `docs/MUX.md`): previously a pane whose process exited on its own was always HELD dead (frozen screen, `(exited N)` cue, `respawn-pane` restart) and nothing auto-closed. With the new default, the death removes the pane at the next reaper pass (within 250 ms) through the kill-pane cascade — a window whose last pane died closes (`%window-close`), an emptied session (and workspace) goes with it, and the daemon exits-when-empty once no clients remain, exactly as if the pane had been `kill-pane`d. `respawn-pane` on a removed pane answers "no such pane". Set `[daemon] remain-on-exit = true` in the config file (live via `reload-config`) to restore the old hold behavior — including the attach client's `(exited N — C-b r respawns)` cue, which only appears for held panes.
- **Scrollback storage: one boxed row per line instead of one flat growing `Vec<Cell>`** (`src/grid/mod.rs`, `src/grid/scroll.rs`, `src/grid/erase.rs`, `src/grid/snapshot.rs`, `src/mux/persist.rs`, `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`, `docs/ADVANCED_FEATURES.md`, `docs/API_REFERENCE.md`): the scrollback buffer was a single flat `Vec<Cell>` in row-major order behind a rotated ring head — a 10k-line × 330-col scrollback held one ~183 MB allocation (with a ~2× doubling transient while growing) and every width resize rebuilt the whole buffer cell by cell. Storage is now `Vec<Box<[Cell]>>` (one small allocation per retained line at its own width) with a plain oldest-first queue; eviction drops one small allocation, and **resize no longer touches scrollback at all** — width changes reflow only the visible screen, stored lines keep the width they scrolled off at, and the cost of a resize is bounded by the viewport rows. Behavior notes: (1) scrollback lines are no longer re-wrapped on width change — renderers were already padding short lines (`ffi.rs copy_row`, mux attach renderer, par-term `push_line_from_slice`), so history renders correctly, it just keeps its original line shapes; (2) `GridSnapshot.scrollback_cells` keeps its flat one-line-per-`cols` payload for byte-compatible mux persistence round-trips, but lines are now always stored oldest-first — the `scrollback_start` field is gone (it serialized as `0` in every real snapshot, so old state files restore correctly and no `FORMAT_VERSION` bump is required); (3) `Grid::scrollback_line`/`row`/`row_mut` signatures are unchanged. Pinned by `scrollback_storage_is_per_line_rows` and `resize_with_large_scrollback_preserves_row_allocations` (asserts per-row allocation pointer identity survives both resize paths), plus `scroll_eviction_drops_one_row_not_a_buffer_rebuild` and `snapshot_round_trips_per_line_storage` (`src/grid/tests.rs`).

- **par-mux `attach` render mode mis-decoded bracketed paste as keystrokes** (`src/mux/attach/input.rs`, `docs/MUX.md`; feature `attach`): the render client's `InputParser` had no bracketed-paste handling — a host left in DECSET 2004 mode by an earlier app sends `ESC[200~ … ESC[201~` around every paste, and the parser silently dropped both markers while SCANNING THE BODY as keystrokes: embedded `C-b` fed the prefix chord scanner, embedded escape sequences decoded as key events (pasting shell text containing `ESC[3~` pressed Delete in the pane), and a paste split so its leading ESC arrived as a lone one-byte read fragmented further. The parser now treats a `ESC[200~`-opened paste as an opaque byte run: the body passes through verbatim as plain bytes (text the user meant to insert, not keystrokes to decode), the markers are consumed, an unterminated paste stops holding the input stream after 1 MiB (the OSC 11 give-up shape, resized), and paste state persists across feeds so a paste split over any read boundary reassembles untouched. Passthrough mode never parsed stdin and was never affected. Pinned by `paste_pair_decodes_to_one_opaque_run`, `paste_body_split_across_feeds_reassembles_untouched`, `byte_at_a_time_decode_follows_the_design`, `paste_marker_splits_at_every_byte_boundary`, `burst_interleaves_paste_chord_and_plain_runs`, and `unterminated_paste_flushes_at_the_cap` (`src/mux/attach/input.rs`).
- **`par-mux --restart` can no longer restore nothing** (intermittent "fresh daemon came up empty"; `src/mux/server.rs`, `src/bin/par_mux/main.rs`, `src/mux/persist.rs`, `docs/MUX.md`): the old daemon unlinked its socket inside the accept-loop teardown, BEFORE `run_persisting`'s final state save — so `--stop`/`--restart`'s wait for the socket to stop accepting could return while the save was still in flight, and the fresh daemon's restore read a missing or stale state file. The socket is now released only after the final save lands (`MuxServer::run_persisting` unlinks via the new `release_socket_path` after `save_off_lock`; embedded `run()` unlinks immediately after its loop), making the documented handoff contract ("the state save the stop just completed is what it restores") actually enforced. A restart whose restore will be empty is now LOUD on both sides: the pre-fork peek (`persist::peek_state`, a read-only mirror of `load_or_quarantine`'s conclusions including the last-good fallback) tells the caller on the terminal whether the fresh daemon will restore nothing — and why (no saved state / previous daemon saved an empty tree / unreadable state), and the detached fresh daemon's stderr is routed to `<state file>.log` beside the state file (created `0600`, appended across restarts, parent dir auto-created) instead of `/dev/null`, so a post-fork startup failure (bind error, restore failure, exit-when-empty) leaves evidence on disk; the empty restore itself logs `restored tree is empty; daemon exiting (exit-when-empty)` in that log, and every exit-when-empty logs `tree empty; daemon exiting (exit-when-empty)`. On Windows, where restart serves in-process with the caller's stderr intact, only the pre-fork announcements and the exit-when-empty log line apply. Pinned by `cli_restart_restores_a_populated_tree_five_trials` (five real-daemon restarts), `cli_restart_after_emptying_announces_the_empty_restore` (both loud lines asserted), and `cli_restart_surfaces_a_fresh_daemon_startup_failure` (a post-fork bind failure is readable in the daemon log) in `tests/mux_restart.rs`.
- **par-mux `send-keys -l` multi-token behavior documented and pinned, not changed — it already matches real tmux** (`src/mux/command.rs`, `tests/mux_send_keys.rs`, `docs/MUX.md`): the card reported `send-keys -t %0 -l echo LEFT` typing `echoLEFT` as a bug, but a probe against real tmux 3.7c shows tmux does the same (`send-keys -t 0 -l echo LEFT` types `echoLEFT`; quoted `-l 'echo LEFT'` types `echo LEFT`; default mode `abc def` types `abcdef`). Tokens join with NOTHING in every mode. par-mux already implements this exactly; the card closes on documentation instead of a code change: MUX.md's `send-keys` row and bullet now state the measured joining rule with the quoted-run and `-H` hex forms as the lossless paths, and the contract is pinned by `send_keys_literal_tokens_join_with_nothing_like_tmux` (`src/mux/command.rs`) and the live-daemon tests `literal_multi_token_payload_drops_the_separator_like_tmux` / `quoted_literal_run_keeps_its_space_like_tmux` (`tests/mux_send_keys.rs`).
- **par-mux `attach` passthrough raced its status draw against pane output** (`src/mux/attach/mod.rs`, `tests/mux_attach.rs`, `docs/MUX.md`; feature `attach`): the passthrough status draw wrapped itself in ESC7/ESC8 (cursor save/restore) and relied on the restore for the post-draw cursor position. Pane output flowing while the draw ran broke that: a pane scroll landing between save and restore left the restored position one line off, and every later output painted over the wrong row (observed on Terminal.app as a fresh prompt overwriting the middle of the previous line). The passthrough session now keeps a shadow emulator of the pane (the render mode's `PaneEmulator`, seeded by the `refresh-client` replay in `resync()` and fed every `%output` chunk in `handle_event`, re-fit on host resize), and the draw keeps the ESC7/ESC8 wrap (which still protects against output interleaved WITHIN the draw) but CLOSES with an absolute CUP at the emulator's tracked cell (`ESC[<row>;<col>H`) — a scroll landing between the draw and the placement cannot make a fresh absolute position wrong. The DECSTBM reserve semantics are unchanged (the content region stays reserved after the draw). Over a held-dead pane the tracked cell is the frozen screen's cell, so placement still targets the right spot; on resize the emulator re-fits and its clamped cursor is what placement reads. Pinned headless by `status_draw_places_the_cursor_at_the_tracked_cell` and `status_draw_tracks_the_frozen_cell_when_the_pane_is_dead` (`src/mux/attach/mod.rs`: the tracked-cell CUP closes every draw, the wrap and DECSTBM reserve survive, the dead pane's frozen cell is placed), and PTY-level by `status_draw_tracks_the_cursor_under_output_flood` (`tests/mux_attach.rs`, unix: a scrolling flood runs while the client's status draws land, every captured draw block closes with its placement CUP — a bare-ESC8 close is the raced shape — and an echo typed after the flood round-trips intact).

### Added
- **par-mux daemon discovery: `--list-servers` and the attach multi-server guard** (`src/mux/discovery.rs` new, `src/mux/mod.rs`, `src/bin/par_mux/main.rs`, `tests/mux_cli.rs`, `docs/MUX.md`): `par-mux --list-servers` enumerates every par-mux daemon this user can reach, so attaching no longer requires remembering socket paths. Each daemon writes a pointer file into its state directory at bind (`<state-base>/servers/<socket-stem>.json`: full socket path, pid, start timestamp, build stamp — tmp-then-rename atomic, re-registration over a live same-socket entry is a byte-for-byte no-op, a dead or foreign-socket entry is replaced) and removes it on the clean shutdown path after the final state save; a crash's stale entry is pruned by the next enumeration, which probes every registered socket (plus — only when the base is the platform default, keeping `--state-dir`-scoped runs hermetic — the named-default socket directory, so named daemons an unregistered binary serves still appear) with `version` + `list-sessions` through the shared `MuxClient` transport and prints one line per live server: `<name-or-path>  sessions=N  <stamp>`, pruning reported on stderr. `par-mux attach` with no NAME and no `--socket` while multiple servers are live refuses with that list plus a `attach to one with par-mux attach <name|path>` hint (exit 1) instead of silently picking one; exactly one live server is attached directly (the resolved socket is pre-set on `AttachOptions`, so the attach module needs no change). Windows named-pipe friendliness comes free: the registry is plain files, the probe rides `connect_local_stream`, and nothing in discovery is unix-only. Pinned by the registry unit suite in `src/mux/discovery.rs` (8 tests: pid liveness, stem-keyed registration, live no-op, dead and foreign-socket replacement, unregister matching, name classification, display labels) and the live-daemon CLI tests in `tests/mux_cli.rs` (`list_servers_shows_both_running_daemons_with_session_counts`: two daemons, one list, session counts, clean-shutdown removal, kill-path pruning; `list_servers_prunes_only_dead_entries_and_reregistration_is_a_noop`; `list_servers_with_nothing_running_reports_empty`; `attach_with_multiple_live_servers_refuses_with_a_list`). Commit `<this>`.
- **par-mux `attach`: window/pane management chords — split `%`/`"`, kill `x`, new-window `c`** (`src/mux/config.rs`, `src/mux/attach/mod.rs`, `src/mux/attach/render.rs`, `tests/mux_attach.rs`, `docs/MUX.md`; feature `attach`): tmux muscle memory now works over an attach. After the prefix: `%` sends `split-window -t <focused> -h` and `"` sends `split-window -t <focused>` (the bare/default direction); both land the pump on the NEW pane — the daemon's reply body IS the fresh pane id, and the landing rides the same select-then-refresh contract every switch follows (passthrough: `select-pane` + `refresh-client` resync; render: focus + re-seed from the fresh `%layout-change`), so the redraw is the new pane's authoritative screen and the next keystrokes reach it. `x` sends `kill-pane -t <focused>`: the pump follows the window's surviving active pane (`list-panes` survivor lookup, the same land-on-the-successor move the switch chords make); killing the window's last pane engages the existing held-dead guard instead (typing dropped, `(exited ? — C-b r respawns)` cue — though the pane is gone, not held, so the honest exit from there is prefix `d`). `c` sends `new-window -t <focused-pane's-session>` (reply = the fresh window id), selects it, and attaches to its active pane. All four keys are settable per chord in the config file's `[client]` table (`split-right`, `split-down`, `kill-pane`, `new-window`; tmux spelling or a literal, parsed with the ONE `parse_prefix` grammar every other chord uses; defaults `%`, `"`, `x`, `c`), rebind live on the reload chord (the same pure `reload_client_chords` pass startup resolves them through — one grammar, one error shape; a malformed chord fails the attach or flashes the reload error). Chords matched by byte BEFORE the fixed command table, so a remapped key never collides with the static arms; an unbound key is consumed silently as before. Pinned headless by `split_right_chord_sends_split_window_and_lands_on_the_new_pane`, `split_down_chord_sends_the_vertical_split_spelling`, `kill_chord_sends_kill_pane_and_lands_on_the_survivor`, `kill_of_the_last_pane_engages_the_dead_guard`, `new_window_chord_follows_the_new_windows_active_pane`, `chord_override_remaps_the_split_chord`, and `management_chord_override_errors_on_malformed_spelling` (`src/mux/attach/mod.rs`) plus `management_chords_parse_route_and_error` (`src/mux/config.rs`); PTY-level by `split_chord_lands_the_client_on_the_new_pane` and `kill_chord_lands_the_client_on_the_survivor` (`tests/mux_attach.rs`, unix). Commit `<this>`.
- **par-mux `attach --mode render`: visible cursor, obvious focus indicator, and a host-background frame fill** (`src/mux/attach/render.rs`, `tests/mux_attach.rs`, `docs/MUX.md`, feature `attach`): the three render-mode gaps the owner's manual pass reported. **Cursor:** each frame now places the host cursor at the FOCUSED pane's tracked cell — the pane emulator's cursor position mapped through the pane's rect origin and the client scroll offset — emitting CUP + DECSCUSR (`CSI Ps SP q`, the pane's tracked shape; blink variants keep blinking) + DECTCEM (`CSI ?25 h/l`): a pane that hid its cursor keeps it hidden, and a scrolled-off-live view (wheel / prefix-[) hides the host cursor entirely, since the live cell is not on screen; the session re-emits only on a state change or after a flushed frame (whose per-cell CUPs moved the host cursor). **Focus indicator:** the focused pane's boundary dividers draw bright-cyan (indexed 14) plus bold — the shipped bold/dim-only highlight was indistinguishable on the owner's terminal — while unfocused dividers stay dim with the default foreground. **Background fill:** every frame cell carries the host terminal's resolved background before painting (`PaneRenderer::set_background`, dark `rgb:0/0/0` default matching the handshake's unprobed client-colors default), so unwritten cells — rows below the layout, short history lines, wide-char spacers — render as background instead of the ratatui default the buffer was born with (the light-grey bands a dark-theme host showed); painted cells keep the core grid's own bg. The OSC 10/11 probe plugs into `set_background` once `conn.rs` reports a resolved host background. Pinned by `focused_cursor_maps_cell_plus_origin_and_hides_when_scrolled`, `focused_cursor_carries_the_tracked_decscusr_shape`, `focused_divider_carries_accent_vs_dim_unfocused`, `frame_fills_every_cell_with_the_configured_background`, and `frame_replaces_cursor_after_flush_and_quiets_when_idle` (`src/mux/attach/render.rs`); the PTY-level screen-reconstruction helper skips the new cursor-placement sequences (`tests/mux_attach.rs`). Commit `<this>`.
- **par-mux configuration file with live reload** (`src/mux/config.rs` new, `src/mux/command.rs`, `src/mux/dispatch.rs`, `src/mux/server.rs`, `src/mux/attach/mod.rs`, `src/mux/attach/render.rs`, `src/bin/par_mux/main.rs`, `tests/mux_cli.rs`, `docs/MUX.md`; feature `mux-bin`/`attach`): `par-mux` now reads `<config dir>/par-mux/config.toml` (or `$PAR_MUX_CONFIG`) for client defaults (attach `prefix`, default `mode`, the `reload` chord) and daemon defaults (`socket`, `state-dir`, `pane-endpoints`, `expose-control-socket`), with flags > env > file > built-in defaults precedence. Three reload entry points, no restart: the `reload-config` control command (no args; one reply line per `[daemon]` setting — `unchanged:`/`restart-required:`; the file speaks only for settings it states, so a daemon started with one-shot `--socket`/`--state-dir` flags stays quiet), the attach reload chord (default `C-b C-r`, itself configurable as `[client] reload`; rebinds prefix/reload LIVE in both passthrough and render mode, flashes `config reloaded`/the error on the status row, and sends `reload-config` so the daemon-side report rides the same keypress — the chord's key is matched by byte, so it never collides with prefix `r`/respawn), and `--gen-config [--force]` (writes the effective settings as TOML, refusing to overwrite without `--force`; `PAR_MUX_CONFIG` redirects the path — the tests never touch the user's real config dir). A present-but-broken config file warns-and-defaults at startup (the state-file D3.2 rule) but is an `%error` on reload: an edit mistake surfaces, never silently ignored. Startup-only in v1 (reported restart-required): `client.mode` (needs a re-attach), `daemon.socket`, `daemon.state-dir` (the persist worker's path is fixed at bind — the spec's tentative LIVE claim for state-dir did not hold and is documented startup-only), `daemon.pane-endpoints`/`expose-control-socket`. Pinned by the parse/precedence/round-trip/chord-parsing suites in `src/mux/config.rs` (10 tests), the dispatch report tests (`reload_config_reports_restart_required_per_changed_setting`, `reload_config_without_an_applied_copy_errors`, `reload_config_rejects_positionals` in `src/mux/dispatch.rs`), the live-daemon CLI round-trips (`reload_config_command_reaches_a_live_daemon`, `gen_config_writes_effective_config_and_refuses_overwrite` in `tests/mux_cli.rs`), and the live-rebind unit test (`reload_chord_rebinds_prefix_and_sends_reload_config` in `src/mux/attach/mod.rs`: the NEW prefix detaches after the chord, the old one forwards, `reload-config` on the wire). Commit `<this>`.

## [0.58.1] - 2026-10-02

### Added
