- **par-mux `attach`: ship-gate integration coverage for resize re-fit and daemon zoom (render mode)** (`tests/mux_attach.rs`, `docs/MUX.md`, `docs/MANUAL-PASS.md` new, feature `attach`): two new PTY-level tests close the render-mode coverage gaps. `render_mode_host_resize_refits_window_layout_and_status_row` resizes the harness PTY master mid-session (the SIGWINCH shape) and asserts the full re-fit chain: the client reports the new grid with the content height, the daemon re-divides the window (`pane-info` ground truth at the new geometry), the renderer re-seeds both panes and repaints them at their re-divided rects, and the status row survives the repaint flood at the host's new bottom row (screen reconstructed from the client's per-cell diff stream). `render_mode_daemon_zoom_shows_single_pane_and_unzoom_restores_split` zooms the focused pane with the daemon's `resize-pane -Z`, asserts the visible-layout re-render (the zoomed pane's content repaints, the true layout keeps both panes under the window), then unzooms and asserts the hidden pane's restore repaint plus `capture-pane` ground truth through the round-trip. The test harness keeps its PTY master so tests can drive host-side resizes. `docs/MANUAL-PASS.md` is the human checklist for the ship gate's manual integration criterion: exact commands to build, start a daemon, seed sessions, and attach in both modes, what to eyeball per terminal (fidelity / resize / mouse / scroll / status bar / zoom / detach cleanliness) in Ghostty, iTerm2, Terminal.app, and over SSH, with a results table. Commit `747172f` (with the test-binding fix `547cd9e`).
- **par-mux `attach`: Phase B status bar and prefix-[ scroll mode (render mode)** (`src/mux/attach/status.rs` new, `src/mux/attach/render.rs`, `tests/mux_attach.rs`, `docs/MUX.md`, feature `attach`): the render-mode bottom row is now the full ratatui status bar — sessions (the shown one bold), the shown session's windows (active bold and `*`-marked), the focused pane's title, and the agent roster as `agent:state` chips — painted into a one-row buffer and flushed as a per-cell diff so a status change writes only the changed cells. The state is the throttled re-query the client contract teaches: `%agent-state-changed` / `%agent-released` / `%agent-telemetry-changed` / `%sessions-changed` (plus renames and active-window moves) mark it stale, and one refresh per event burst re-runs `list-sessions` / targeted `list-windows` / `pane-title` / `list-agents`; when the shown window's session is gone, the view ends cleanly (the `%sessions-changed` client contract). `prefix [` enters a keyboard scroll viewport over the focused pane's client scrollback (NOT tmux copy-mode — no search/copy/selection): the view holds against pane output (offset clamps to the new extent instead of snapping), moves with Up/Down (line), PgUp/PgDn (page), Home (history top), and exits with q / Enter / End snapping back to live; keys never reach the pane while it is up, and the status bar shows a bold `[scroll +N]` cue. Session/window switching reuses the passthrough dispatch semantics: prefix `o`/`n`/`p`/`(`/`)` are daemon-side selects followed by a full renderer re-seed (fresh layout report + per-pane replays) and a status re-query. The daemon window renders into the rows above the status row, so size reports carry the content height and a host resize repaints the row with the same redraw discipline as the pane frames. Pinned by the status unit suite in `status.rs` (composition facts, bold marking, scroll cue, truncation, row diffing, tail erasure), the renderer scroll-viewport tests in `render.rs` (hold-against-output, exit snap, no-scrollback refusal, history painting), and the PTY-level `render_mode_status_bar_shows_sessions_and_updates_on_agent_changes` + `render_mode_prefix_bracket_enters_scroll_mode_and_q_exits` (`tests/mux_attach.rs`). Commit `d4c05b6` (the third Listener-import gating in `0eb8a9f`).
- **par-mux `attach`: Phase B input router (render mode)** (`src/mux/attach/input.rs`, `src/mux/attach/render.rs`, `src/bin/par_mux/main.rs`, feature `attach`): `par-mux attach --mode render` is now CLI-selectable (passthrough stays the default and an unknown `--mode` value falls back to it), and the render session's stdin is routed rather than forwarded. An incremental hand-rolled stdin tokenizer (`mux::attach::input::InputParser` — crossterm's parser is crate-private and its reader would race the client's own stdin thread) splits the host's byte stream into plain runs (forwarded verbatim behind the prefix scan), decodable key sequences, and SGR mouse reports. Key sequences re-encode against the focused pane's tracked input state via the shared `keyboard::encode_key` — DECCKM application cursor, kitty flags, modifyOtherKeys, exactly what the pane's replay + `%output` set — so a pane running vim gets `ESC O A` for Up while a plain shell gets `ESC [ A`. Mouse: the host captures SGR+motion tracking; a click locates the pane in the Phase B layout rects, focuses it (locally and daemon-side `select-pane`), and forwards a pane-relative re-encoded SGR report only when that pane owns mouse tracking (its emulator tracks the pane's own DECSET 1000/1002/1003); drag and release forward the same path. Wheel: over a pane that does NOT own mouse mode, the wheel scrolls the CLIENT's view into the pane's scrollback (3 lines/notch, clamped, painted from scrollback with the live screen shifted down; keystrokes snap back to live); over an owning pane it forwards pane-relative. Full kitty keyboard forwarding (negotiating flags, CSI u re-encode) is deferred to a later Phase B card and noted in docs/MUX.md. Pinned by the parser suite in `input.rs` (10 tests), the renderer router tests in `render.rs` (scrollback paint, mouse-ownership gating, rect lookup, DECCKM re-encode through the pane emulator), and the PTY-level `tests/mux_attach.rs` render-mode tests (arrow re-encoding verified against the pane's own capture, wheel scrollback, mouse forwarding pane-relative through a live daemon). Commit `68373f2` (status-row redraw after startup repaint floods and host resizes in `61cff0d`).
- **par-mux `attach`: Phase B layout parser and pane renderer** (`src/mux/attach/layout.rs`, `src/mux/attach/render.rs`, feature `attach`, now with ratatui 0.30): the rendering foundation the full attach TUI builds on, behind a mode seam that leaves Phase A untouched. The layout parser maps a `%layout-change` triple (`<layout> <visible-layout> <flags>`) to absolute per-pane rects — the `Z` flag renders the visible (zoomed) layout, empty triples (bare `%window-add`) parse as no panes, malformed strings error with a byte offset. Golden tests over real-tmux layout strings plus round-trips against the daemon's own `LayoutTree::render`/`geometry` pin the same leaf order `list-panes -t` reports. The renderer mirrors every visible pane in its own core `Terminal` fed by the `refresh-client -t` replays and `%output` bytes, maps cells to a ratatui `Buffer` (truecolor passthrough, SGR attributes, wide-char spacers marked skip in the diff, combining marks joined into one symbol), draws dividers with a focused-pane highlight (UTF-8 box drawing with an ACS `| - +` fallback), and damage-diffs frames at a 16 ms cadence — an output flood between two frames coalesces into one diff, pinned by the flood test. Passthrough stays the default (`run`); `run_with_mode(AttachMode::Render)` selects the renderer. Pinned by the unit suites in both modules and `render_mode_layout_and_pane_replay_match_daemon_ground_truth` (`tests/mux_attach.rs`: a live daemon's split layout string parses to the daemon's rects and each pane's replay reproduces its `capture-pane` ground truth). Commit `953ae0e` (Windows import gating in `9d7c678`).
- **par-mux `attach`: Phase A passthrough client** (`src/mux/attach/`, `src/bin/par_mux/main.rs`, feature `attach`): `par-mux attach [-t TARGET] [--prefix KEY]` puts one pane fullscreen in your terminal — the host terminal is the pane's VT emulator. The client runs the documented handshake, resolves the target (pane id; window/session via the targeted list queries; no `-t` = newest session's newest pane), writes the pane's screen-restore replay to stdout verbatim, and forwards `%output` raw. Stdin flows back in chunked `send-keys -H` (~512-byte chunks) behind a `C-b` prefix (`--prefix` overrides): `d` detaches (terminal restored, exit 0, pane keeps running), `o`/arrows cycle panes, `n`/`p` and `(`/`)` switch windows/sessions, `r` respawns a held-dead pane — each switch a daemon-side `select-*` plus `refresh-client` resync redraw. A DECSTBM-reserved bottom status row shows session, pane title, agent count, and an `(exited N)` cue. On `%exit`/socket close the client does one reconnect-and-requery before reporting the loss. Mouse tracking and graphics protocols work in passthrough (pane bytes flow through, host reports flow back). Pinned by the unit suite in `src/mux/attach/mod.rs` (FakeDaemon wire framing) and `tests/mux_attach.rs` (the client under a real PTY: byte fidelity, prefix detach, resync switching, clean restore). Commit `ff46b96`, building on the scaffolding commit `663be0c` (with the Windows-side stdin-thread and import fixes `92a35c6` and `bd5ebf8`).
- **par-mux `refresh-client` accepts the `-t`-less size-report form** (`src/mux/command.rs`, `src/mux/dispatch.rs`): the attach handshake's `refresh-client -C WxH -p WxH` (no `-t`) previously `%error`ed, so the size report never landed. `RefreshClient`'s pane is now optional: the target-less form is a pure size report — `-p` applies daemon-wide exactly as before and `-C` resizes the newest session's active window (the same stand-in bare `new-window` uses) — and never replays a pane screen; the replay stays bound to the `-t` form. Pinned by `parses_target_less_refresh_client_as_a_pure_size_report` (`src/mux/command.rs`), `target_less_refresh_client_sizes_the_newest_active_window`, `target_less_refresh_client_with_no_sessions_errors_cleanly`, and `target_less_refresh_client_never_replays_a_screen` (`src/mux/dispatch.rs`).
- **par-mux `list-windows -t <session>` and `list-panes -t <window>`** (`src/mux/command.rs`, `src/mux/dispatch.rs`): the attach path's tree queries. `list-windows -t` replies one line per window of that session in session order as the fixed positional shape `@N <marker> <name>` (`*` marks the session's active window, `-` the rest; the name is the line remainder, so a spaced name survives). `list-panes -t` replies one line per pane of that window in layout leaf order as `%N <leaf> <marker>` — `leaf` is the deterministic index a client maps tmux layout-string leaves to pane ids by, and the same marker marks the window's active pane. Both queries accept typed ids and names; the bare global forms keep their exact pre-existing shapes (`%N` lines, `@N: name` lines), so existing clients are untouched. `list-commands` advertises the new `targeted` feature token on both rows. Fixed shapes, no `-F` — the T4.E decision. Pinned by `targeted_list_windows_and_list_panes_reconstruct_the_tree` (`tests/mux_targets.rs`) and `parses_targeted_list_windows_and_list_panes` (`src/mux/command.rs`). Commit `ed94c97`.

### Fixed
- **par-mux attach render mode: three sidebar/label defects from the manual pass** (`src/mux/attach/render.rs`, `tests/mux_attach.rs`): toggling the side panel now refits the grid through the pump's `resize_to`, whose `repaint_all` erases the region the old layout vacated — before, only changed cells repainted, so ghost pane contents stayed at the pre-toggle positions beside a dead margin the width of the strip (the chord parks a `pending_grid_refit`; the pump performs it and re-queries the strip's sections, which the refit's renderer reconstruction drops). Border labels now paint the daemon's `pane-title` value — the user `-T` label (prefix `$`) when set, else the OSC title — re-queried on seed and every throttled status refresh; before, the painter read the shell's OSC title only, so user labels never showed (pinned by `render_mode_border_label_paints_the_user_title`, which sets labels daemon-side and asserts they paint in the herdr style). And only the ACTIVE workspace's sidebar row carries the full-width inverted block; the shown window's row brightens instead (two blocks read as two actives).
- **par-mux `attach` passthrough printed the help dump on the cursor's line and let later keystrokes paint over it** (`src/mux/attach/mod.rs`): the prefix-`?` dump started at the cursor's current position — the first category header landed on the prompt row — and the pane's cursor never advanced, so every later keystroke mixed into the help text. The dump now leads with a blank line, bolds the category headers (SGR 1, the render modal's accent rows), and advances the pane past the text with empty Enters (~2 rows each, half the dump's row count) so the shell lands below the help; the text stays in the pane's scrollback. Pinned by `passthrough_help_dump_leads_with_a_blank_line_and_bolds_headers` and `help_chord_advances_the_pane_past_the_dump` (`src/mux/attach/mod.rs`).
- **par-mux `attach` passthrough left switched-to windows at their restored size** (`src/mux/attach/mod.rs`): the handshake's target-less `refresh-client -C` sizes only the newest session's active window, and pane switches sent select + replay without a size report — a window restored at 80x24 stayed 80x24 in a tall host, and its apps ran at the stale height (the manual-pass htop report; render mode's switch path always reported). Passthrough `resync` now size-reports the target pane's window at the content region (`cols x rows-1`, the status row stays reserved) before every replay. Pinned by `resync_size_reports_the_target_window_before_the_replay` (`src/mux/attach/mod.rs`).
- **Styled exports replayed default-colored text through the host's palette** (`src/grid/export.rs`, `src/grid/tests.rs`, `src/terminal/tests/terminal_tests.rs`, `src/mux/server.rs`, `tests/mux_reattach.rs`): every styled export (the attach replay's `export_screen_restore_sequence`, `capture-pane -e`, `export_styled_buffer`) emitted the DEFAULT colors as explicit palette entries (`37` fg white / `40` bg black — `Cell::default()`'s values) in each SGR run, so a reattached client's host painted visible grey bands around replayed prompt rows whose true appearance was the pane host's own default background (palette-0 on a themed host is not black). The exporter now omits fg `Named(White)` and bg `Named(Black)` — the default sentinels — from every SGR run, so replays paint default-colored text with the host's own defaults, exactly as the live pane did. Documented conflation: an explicit `ESC[37m`/`ESC[40m` exports as default too (the cell model cannot distinguish them). Pinned by `styled_export_omits_default_colors` (`src/terminal/tests/terminal_tests.rs`), the updated `test_styled_exports_golden_bytes` (`src/grid/tests.rs`), and the updated `-e` capture and reattach assertions.
- **par-mux `attach` passthrough attached with the cursor one row below the prompt** (`src/mux/attach/mod.rs`): the `resync()` seed appended a trailing newline to the daemon's screen-restore byte stream before feeding the shadow emulator — but the stream ends with the pane's real cursor CUP, so the shadow (and every status draw's absolute placement with it) parked one row low until the shell's next repaint. The feed is now the verbatim stream. Pinned by `resync_feeds_the_shadow_the_restore_stream_verbatim` (`src/mux/attach/mod.rs`).
- **par-mux `attach` passthrough mixed pane content with the terminal's prior screen** (`src/mux/attach/mod.rs`, `tests/mux_attach.rs`, `docs/MUX.md`): the startup `refresh-client` replay was written verbatim over whatever the host terminal was already showing. The passthrough client now enters the alternate screen as part of terminal setup (in `TerminalGuard`, right after raw mode, before the replay is written) and leaves it on every exit path — detach, `%exit`, socket close, error, panic unwind — before the DECSTBM restore. The status row's DECSTBM reserve applies within the alt screen unchanged. Render mode already entered its own alt screen; `LeaveAlternateScreen` being idempotent means the guard's leave after the session's own is harmless. Pinned by the PTY-level `detach_restores_the_terminal_region` (now asserting `ESC[?1049h` at startup, its ordering before the DECSTBM reserve, and `ESC[?1049l` in the detach tail on unix; Windows ConPTY re-encodes 1049 switches, so those bytes stay unix-asserted per the existing cfg-split conventions).
- **par-mux `attach` flooded the screen over a held-dead pane** (`src/mux/attach/mod.rs`, `tests/mux_attach.rs`, `docs/MUX.md`): after the focused pane's shell exited (remain-on-exit), every keystroke was forwarded as `send-keys`, which both echoed the bytes into the pane's frozen screen and provoked the daemon's pinned NotStartedError reply per keypress. The client now drops plain stdin bytes while `exited` is set (the flag it already tracks from `%pane-exited` / `pane-info` for the status cue); prefix chords keep working, so detach and respawn stay reachable, and prefix `r` (`respawn-pane`) clears the flag and resumes forwarding on `%pane-respawned`. The status row's `(exited N)` cue now names the chord: `(exited N — C-b r respawns)`. Pinned headless by `dead_pane_takes_no_stdin_bytes_but_prefix_keys_still_route`, `status_line_names_the_respawn_chord_when_the_pane_is_dead`, and `respawn_chord_resumes_forwarding_after_revival` (`src/mux/attach/mod.rs`), and PTY-level by `dead_pane_takes_no_typing_and_prefix_r_respawns` (`tests/mux_attach.rs`, unix: the typed burst never echoes, no error text renders, prefix `r` round-trips a fresh echo through the revived pane).
- **par-mux `attach --mode render` with no `-t` failed to resolve a session** (`src/mux/attach/render.rs`, `src/mux/attach/mod.rs`, `tests/mux_attach.rs`, `docs/MUX.md`): `list-sessions` replies `$N: name`, and the render client's target-less resolution took the whole whitespace token (`$0:`) — the daemon's id parser rejects the trailing colon, so `list-windows -t $0:` errored and the client exited 1 with "the session has no windows" instead of the documented default. The resolution now parses the id at the colon (a shared `parse_session_line` helper, the same rule status.rs and passthrough's own re-query used) and picks the NEWEST session (highest id — the deterministic newest stand-in), then its active window's active pane. The same colon-bearing tokenization in both `switch_session` implementations (passthrough and render) is fixed too: their `$N:` tokens could never match the colon-less `session_id` the status view records, so `prefix (` / `)` broke with two or more sessions. Pinned by `target_less_resolution_takes_newest_session_without_colon` (scripted daemon, `src/mux/attach/render.rs`), `parse_session_line_strips_the_sigil_colon` (`src/mux/attach/mod.rs`), and the PTY-level `render_mode_without_target_attaches_the_newest_session` + `render_mode_without_target_picks_the_newest_session` (`tests/mux_attach.rs`). Commit `6a6008d`.
- **`PtySession`'s reader thread never signaled a quiet child death and could spin on a dead pty** (`src/pty_session/reader.rs`): the EOF arm stored `running = false` and notified waiters but bumped no generation counter, so a frontend sleep-polling `update_generation()` never observed a child that exited without producing output; and a terminal read error was logged and retried, so on Linux (where a dead pty's master read returns EIO, not EOF) the reader looped on the error forever. Both arms now exit through one `reader_dead` helper — `running = false`, condvar notify, then the generation bump (notify first, so a blocked `wait_for_update` waiter wakes on the running flag; the bump after is what a generation-poll observer sees on its next poll) — and a `read_error_is_terminal` classifier treats BrokenPipe and EIO (unix) as fatal while keeping WouldBlock/Interrupted/UnexpectedEof retryable. The Windows ConPTY contract is untouched (liveness still comes from `try_wait` polling; the pipe never EOFs). Pinned by `quiet_child_death_advances_the_generation`, `injected_terminal_read_error_stops_the_reader_without_spinning`, and `read_error_classifier_treats_broken_pipe_and_eio_as_terminal` (`src/pty_session/reader.rs`). Commit `188210e`.
### Changed
- **`Cell` slimmed from 56 to 40 bytes** (`src/cell.rs`): fg/bg/underline colors are packed into `u32` words (2-bit variant tag + compact payload: named = 4-bit index, indexed = 8-bit, RGB = 24-bit; the underline `Option` shares its top bit as a presence flag) and the combining-mark store moved from an inline `SmallVec<[char; 4]>` (24 bytes in every cell) to a null-optimized `Option<Arc<Vec<char>>>` (8 bytes; cells with combining marks now share the mark buffer on clone instead of deep-copying it). All `Cell` accessor signatures and the public `Color`/`CellFlags` types are unchanged, so the rlib API stays semver-minor — the par-term frontend compiles against the new core with only a version-pin bump. Scrollback memory for a 10k-line × 80-col buffer drops from ~46MB of `Cell` storage to ~32MB (56B → 40B per cell), with combining-mark sequences paying one small heap allocation each.
- **`Cell`'s serialized shape is unchanged, but is now produced by manual serde impls** (`src/cell.rs`): the derive on the struct was replaced by an `into`/`from` wire-format mirror (`CellSerde`) so packing does not alter the JSON — mux persistence files and replay snapshots stay byte-compatible in both directions with 0.58.0 output (verified field-for-field against the pre-change derive). Treat any hand-written deserializer that matched the old derive output as still-valid; none needed.
- **par-mux workspaces: a first-class level above sessions** (`src/mux/tree/`, `src/mux/command.rs`, `src/mux/dispatch.rs`, `src/mux/server.rs`, `src/mux/persist.rs`, `docs/MUX.md`). The hierarchy is now daemon > workspace > session > window > pane. A session belongs to exactly one workspace for its life; each workspace tracks its own active session, and the daemon tracks an active workspace. New commands: `new-workspace [-n name]` (creates and selects, replying the `+N` id), `list-workspaces` (`+N: name` lines, the active one suffixed ` active`), `select-workspace -t <workspace>` (resumes that workspace's previously active session), `rename-workspace -t <workspace> <name>`, and `kill-workspace -t <workspace>` (kills every session, window, and pane in it). `new-session` gains `-t <workspace>`; a bare `new-session` lands in the active workspace, lazily creating a default one named `main`. `list-sessions` gains `-t <workspace>` and its reply shape is now workspace-aware: each line is `+W: wname: $N: name` (bare = every workspace). Workspaces use the `+` id sigil. A workspace dies when its last session dies; exit-when-empty is unchanged in substance — empty means no sessions across all workspaces, so an empty workspace never keeps the daemon alive. A new argument-less `%workspaces-changed` broadcast (the `%sessions-changed` convention) fires on workspace add, kill, rename, select, and on any session cascade that removes an emptied workspace; `list-commands` carries the new rows with the `workspace` feature token on `new-session`/`list-sessions`. Persistence: `FORMAT_VERSION` is now **3** — the save carries workspaces, their membership, and the active pointers, plus a fourth id counter. NOT backward compatible by design (workspaces are first-class, no migration): pre-workspace (v2) state files are quarantined and the daemon starts fresh. Pinned by the workspace tree tests (`src/mux/tree/tests.rs`), dispatch/broadcast tests (`src/mux/server.rs`), persistence round-trip and v2-quarantine tests (`src/mux/persist.rs`), and the live-daemon tests `workspace_commands_over_the_wire` (`tests/mux_daemon.rs`) and `workspaces_survive_a_restart` (`tests/mux_restart.rs`).

## [0.58.0] - 2026-10-01

### Added
- **par-mux preserves held-dead panes across a daemon restart** (ARC-114; `src/mux/persist.rs`, `src/mux/pane.rs`, `docs/MUX.md`): a pane held dead at save time is now restored held dead instead of being respawned as a fresh shell. `PersistPane` gains `dead` and `exit_code` (both `serde(default, skip_serializing_if)`, so `FORMAT_VERSION` is unchanged and older files and older daemons degrade to the previous respawn behavior). `MuxPane::mark_dead_with_code` records a persisted code without asking the (absent) child, and `PaneFactory::create_dead_pane` builds the processless pane (**breaking for external `PaneFactory` implementors**: no default impl, because a pane's session is non-optional). A restored dead pane keeps its exit code, spawn command, agent identity and frozen screen (re-hung without `restore_for_new_process`'s mode and alternate-screen reset), reports `exited=<code>` in `pane-info`, replays `%pane-exited` at registration, is never re-announced by the reaper, and restarts with `respawn-pane` without `-k`. Live panes restore exactly as before. Pinned by `a_held_dead_pane_round_trips_as_held_dead` and `a_save_file_without_the_dead_fields_loads_alive` (`src/mux/persist.rs`), and the dead-pane restart tests in `tests/mux_restart.rs`.
- **par-mux `pane-exited-replay`** (ENH-042; `src/mux/command.rs`, `src/mux/dispatch.rs`, `src/mux/server.rs`): a read-only control command that replays the held panes' `%pane-exited %N [code]` lines on demand to the issuing client only, queued ahead of an empty reply block, framed exactly like registration replay. It reuses the new `replay_pane_exited_lines` helper that registration replay now also calls, so the two cannot drift. Pinned by `pane_exited_replay_reaches_only_the_issuing_client` and `pane_exited_replay_with_no_held_panes_is_empty` (`tests/mux_daemon.rs`).
- **FFI scroll-aware damage (ENH-038, ABI v5 additive)**: `TermScrollDelta` + `ptec_terminal_scroll_delta_since` / `ptec_terminal_content_dirty_ranges_since` let a renderer blit on scroll and redraw only content-changed rows. `TERM_CORE_ABI_VERSION` is now 5; see FFI_GUIDE's Scroll-Aware Damage section for why `total_lines_scrolled` was rejected as a damage source.
- **par-mux `clear-history`** (`9b1b1ed`; `src/mux/command.rs`, `src/mux/dispatch.rs`): `clear-history -t %N` clears a daemon pane's scrollback and wipes its visible screen through the emulator's own ED 3 path (graphics teardown and the `ScreenCleared` event included). It is classified structural (its success saves state), because the cleared scrollback is what a restart-restore replays — a content classification would resurrect the history on restart. Pinned by `clear_history_wipes_scrollback_and_screen` (`src/mux/dispatch.rs`).
- **par-mux `list-commands` and registration-time held-state replay** (ENH-037; `80ba80a`, `d92febf`; `src/mux/command.rs`, `src/mux/dispatch.rs`, `src/mux/server.rs`). The new read-only query answers one `name [feature …]` line per dispatchable command, generated from the `COMMANDS` table, whose rows carry the feature tokens (`resize-pane zoom absolute`, `split-window before start-dir`, `respawn-pane kill start-dir`, `pane-info cmd`, `refresh-client cell-pixels`, `capture-pane escape`), plus a daemon-level `features replay-held-state` line — a client learns a daemon's abilities without probing command by command. On registration a client now receives `%pane-exited` per held pane and `%layout-change` (raw flags `Z`) per zoomed window, queued ahead of its first command's reply; the snapshot and the registry join happen under one tree-lock hold (documented lock order: `tree` then `clients`), and a held pane's `%pane-exited` may repeat where the snapshot overlaps an in-flight reap broadcast — idempotent by contract. `%layout-change` rendering is shared between broadcast and replay through `render_layout_change`, so the two forms cannot drift. Pinned by `list_commands_reply_lists_every_command_exactly_once` and `every_command_feature_key_is_a_command_name` (`src/mux/command.rs`), `held_state_replay_reports_held_panes_and_zoomed_windows` (`src/mux/server.rs`).
- **`InlineImage` is constructible and exported from Python** (QA-220; `src/python_bindings/types/graphics.rs`, `python/par_term_emu_core_rust/__init__.py`). The inline-image store's methods were all bound, but the class had no `__init__` and was not re-exported from the package root, so Python could never create the first image the store holds. It now has a positional constructor (`protocol` through `display_rows`, trailing `id=None`) and joins the `par_term_emu_core_rust` exports. Pinned by `tests/test_inline_image.py`.
- **FFI `ptec_terminal_scrollback_total_scrolled`** (part of C ABI v4; it first landed as `terminal_scrollback_total_scrolled` while `TERM_CORE_ABI_VERSION` still read 3, and the v4 bump subsumes it): total lines ever pushed into scrollback, so an embedder mirroring the scrollback window (ParDeck's history view) derives head evictions and tail appends from the `total`/`count` pair instead of re-reading the whole buffer — the count alone freezes at the ring cap. Pinned by `ffi_scrollback_total_scrolled_tracks_the_window` (invariant `count == min(total, cap)`, cap freeze, alt-screen active-grid reads, ED 3J reset). Note for ENH-038: this counter is a window cursor, not a scroll-damage source — the four failure modes listed there are exactly why.
- **par-mux `pane-info` reports held panes** (audit ARC-095; `src/mux/dispatch.rs`): an optional trailing `exited=<code|?>` token, so a client that connects after `%pane-exited` can render remain-on-exit state. `?` means the exit code was unreadable. A live pane's reply is unchanged.
- **`PtySession::terminal_write()`** (QA-195): exclusive terminal access as a `TerminalWriteGuard` that republishes the geometry mirror when dropped, for callers that cannot use the `with_terminal_mut` closure. **`MuxPane::with_terminal_mut`** is the pane-level equivalent.
- **`TmuxNotification.exit_code`** for `pane-exited` (QA-190; `src/python_bindings/types/notification.rs`): the held pane's exit code as an `int`, `None` when it was unreadable (signal death) and for every other notification type. Python clients no longer parse it out of `name`.

- **par-mux opt-in per-pane hook-only endpoints** (ENH-039; `src/mux/server.rs`, `docs/MUX.md`). `par-mux --pane-endpoints` (default off) gives every pane its own socket beside the control socket that accepts only the four hook-report methods bound to that pane: a report naming another pane is refused, a report omitting `pane_id` is filed for the bound pane, and any control command is answered with a hook-only error before the connection closes. `PAR_MUX_SOCKET` then names the pane's endpoint; the full control socket is exported only as `PAR_MUX_CONTROL_SOCKET` under `--expose-control-socket` or `PAR_MUX_CONTROL=1`. Pinned by the daemon-level env-contract matrix in `tests/mux_daemon.rs`.
- **par-mux fuzz targets run in CI** (ARC-121; `fuzz/`, `.github/workflows/fuzz.yml`). The `mux_parse_command` and `mux_hook_report` targets existed but never ran; they join the fuzz matrix, and `make fuzz-all` runs all seven.
- **`make stub-drift`** (QA-204). Rebuilds the extension, regenerates `_native.pyi` and fails on a diff — `stub-check` never regenerated, so stub drift went unnoticed.
- **`make release-check`** (ENH-036; `scripts/check_release_notes.py`). Every `feat`/`fix` commit since the top CHANGELOG section's base tag must be cited there by SHA or card ID, with a `Changelog: skip` trailer as the escape hatch; `--write` maintains the top compare links idempotently.
- **Dependency-audit gate** (ENH-040; `deny.toml`, `make audit-deps`, weekly `deps-audit.yml`). cargo-deny advisory/source/ban policy plus bun and pip-audit runs with fail-closed tool guards; needs network, so it stays out of `checkall`.
- **Offline doc link gate** (ENH-035; `make doc-links-check`). lychee `--offline --include-fragments` over the repo docs fails on broken intra-repo links and GitHub-slug heading anchors; wired into `checkall` and the CI lint job.
- **The FFI surface doc gate grew to five fail-closed checks** (ENH-034; `scripts/check_ffi_surface.py`): exported functions, header typedef names, `TERM_*` constant coverage, ABI-table version agreement, and no stale "hand-written header" claim about the cbindgen-generated `include/terminal_core.h`.
- **The streaming `Connected` message seeds `initial_screen` with the pane's scrollback** (`9b68492`), so a client attaching to a mux-backed streamer sees the pane history from its first frame.
- **Agent roster wire surface + streamer roster watcher** (`6b6a723`..`ed7f3cb`, redial/decode hardening `a2d61a6`; ParDeck RQ5/Q6): the streaming server can now serve the par-mux agent roster to clients. `proto/terminal.proto` grows `AgentEntry`/`AgentRoster`/`AgentStateChanged` (ServerMessage oneof 38/39; `ClientMessage` untouched — the roster is push-only, full snapshot at connect, deltas after). `src/streaming/roster.rs` parses `list-agents` output (tokens 1–4 positional with source validated `hook|scrape`, `reason=` base64-decoded, unknown keys ignored, malformed lines skipped — a malformed line can never panic or emit a half-parsed entry) and translates notifications to deltas on the daemon's real grammar (`%agent-state-changed <pane> <agent> <state>[ source=<hook|scrape>]` — source is an optional key=value tail token, reason is never carried on deltas; `%agent-released`/`%pane-exited` → release). `roster_watcher.rs` runs one notification-driven watcher per streamer (no timers; the only wait is reconnect backoff), keeps a mutex-guarded roster cache, sends the snapshot to every client right after its `Connected` handshake on both connect paths (subscribe-before-snapshot, so no delta is missed), broadcasts deltas server-wide via the new `StreamingServer::broadcast_all` (the dead `Broadcaster` is untouched), and after a daemon loss reconnects, re-snapshots, and broadcasts a fresh roster so no ghost agent survives a daemon restart. A lagged client (broadcast channel overflow) is resynced with a fresh snapshot instead of silently missing deltas. `py_convert.rs` covers the two new variants for the Python side. Read-only daemon access (`list-agents` + notification listen); par-mux still owns every pane.
### Security
- **par-mux restored dead panes keep their OSC 7 host** (SEC-128 follow-up to ARC-114; `src/mux/persist.rs`, `src/mux/pane.rs`, `docs/MUX.md`): a held-dead pane persisted only its OSC 7 directory, so after a daemon restart `respawn-pane` saw a host-less report, treated it as local, and restarted the pane in the remote shell's directory whenever that path existed on this machine. `PersistPane` gains `cwd_host` (`serde(default, skip_serializing_if)`, so `FORMAT_VERSION` is unchanged and files stay compatible in both directions), captured only when the cwd came from the OSC 7 report and re-seeded with the cwd on restore, so the existing remote-host rejection applies. Pinned by `a_dead_panes_remote_osc7_host_survives_restore`, `a_save_file_without_cwd_host_loads_as_before` (`src/mux/persist.rs`) and `respawn_of_a_restored_dead_pane_ignores_its_remote_osc7_cwd` (`tests/mux_restart.rs`).
- **Trigger results, highlights and bookmarks are bounded** (audit QA-197; `src/terminal/trigger.rs`, `src/terminal/semantic_snapshot.rs`). Trigger output could grow without bound: `Notify`, `MarkLine` and `SplitPane` action results ignored the `max_action_results` cap (only `RunCommand`/`PlaySound`/`SendText` enforced it), trigger highlights were never evicted, and a `duration_ms == 0` highlight never expires (`0 = permanent` by design), so a trigger firing on every written line grew memory for the life of the session; `add_bookmark` — also fed by `MarkLine` actions — grew with it. All six result variants now go through one `TriggerState::push_action_result` helper that keeps the already-capped variants' drop-newest-when-full behavior; highlights evict the oldest beyond `max_action_results` (100); bookmarks cap at `MAX_BOOKMARKS` (1000, matching `max_matches`), evicting the oldest. Pinned by `test_trigger_highlight_cap_evicts_oldest`, `test_trigger_markline_result_cap_drops_newest` and `test_add_bookmark_cap_evicts_oldest`.
- **par-mux `pane-info` no longer serves a program-controlled `cmd=` name unfiltered** (audit SEC-129; `src/mux/foreground.rs`). The foreground command name is the basename of argv[0], which any program sets (`exec -a`), so a pane could hand a mirroring client ANSI escapes, a spoofed name, or an ARG_MAX-sized token for its close-confirmation prompt. `ProcessTable::foreground_command` now applies the `git_branch` rule: a name that is empty, over `MAX_FOREGROUND_NAME_LEN` (128 bytes, new caps-table row), or carries a control character is served absent, never truncated or stripped. The name stays a display hint, not an identity. Pinned by `foreground_command_rejects_control_and_oversize_names`.
- **The Python debug log is no longer a shared-temp symlink target** (audit SEC-131, recurring SEC-120; `python/par_term_emu_core_rust/debug.py`). With `DEBUG_LEVEL` set, `DebugLogger` opened the fixed path `<tempdir>/par_term_emu_debug_python.log` with `open(..., "w")`: on a shared `/tmp` another local user could plant a symlink there and have the log truncate and overwrite any file the victim can write, and the log was created world-readable under the default umask. It now matches `src/debug.rs`: the file is `par_term_emu_debug_python_<pid>.log`, created `0600` through `os.open` with `O_NOFOLLOW`, and a planted symlink fails the open, which disables logging (fail closed). **Behavior change:** tools tailing the old fixed filename must glob `par_term_emu_debug_python_*.log`. `debug.DEBUG_FILE` still names the active path. Pinned by `tests/test_debug_log.py`.
- **Kitty `t=t` reads and deletes only the file that passed the temp gate** (`src/graphics/kitty.rs`, audit SEC-130, CWE-367). The gate checked the canonicalized path, but the file was opened and later deleted through the original path, and `O_NOFOLLOW` guards only the final component, so a parent directory swapped for a symlink in between redirected both. The read now opens the canonical path, re-runs the temp-root and filename gate on the path the kernel reports for the open handle (`/proc/self/fd` on Linux, `F_GETPATH` on macOS), and pins the pending delete to the handle's `(dev, ino)`: a file swapped in at that path after the read is left in place. Behavior change: on Linux without `/proc` mounted, `t=t` now fails closed.
- **`cargo audit` is clean** (`Cargo.toml`; SEC-134). The `image` dependency's `rayon` feature is dropped — nothing in this crate used it — so the unmaintained `paste` proc-macro (RUSTSEC-2024-0436) and its chain (`pulp`, `rav1e`, `exr`, `ravif`) leave Cargo.lock entirely.


### Changed
- **par-mux commands are one declarative table** (ARC-094b; `src/mux/command.rs`, `scripts/check_mux_docs.py`). Each `COMMANDS` row is now `(name, parser, feature-tokens)`, so a command's parser and its `list-commands` metadata live on the same row instead of two parallel name-keyed lists; the ENH-033 doc gate's table parser follows the row shape.
- **C ABI v4: the breaking FFI revision** (audit decision D4; ARC-101, ARC-112, ARC-114; **breaking for C/Swift embedders**). `TERM_CORE_ABI_VERSION` and `ptec_terminal_abi_version()` are now 4. One revision instead of three; migration notes are in `docs/FFI_GUIDE.md` ("ABI Version").
  - **Every export is prefixed `ptec_`** (ARC-112): `terminal_create` → `ptec_terminal_create`, and so on for all 22 functions, so the C surface cannot collide with another library's `terminal_*` symbols in one process. Types (`SharedCell`, `TerminalObserverVtable`, …) and `TERM_*` constants keep their names.
  - **`SharedCell` colors are display colors** (ARC-101). They used the fixed xterm table, so after `OSC 4;1;rgb:00/00/ff` SGR 31 still read back as (128,0,0). They now resolve like the Python snapshot: the live ANSI palette for the 16 base colors, the OSC 10/11 default for a default cell, and bold brightening. `attrs` gains `TERM_ATTR_DEFAULT_FG` (bit 12), `TERM_ATTR_DEFAULT_BG` (bit 13) and `TERM_ATTR_HAS_COMBINING` (bit 14). Default is recognized by value: a color equal to the unstyled color or the current OSC 10/11 value. An explicit SGR 37/40 (or `38;5;7`/`48;5;0`) is flagged the same way, and a cell written under an earlier OSC 10/11 value keeps that color unflagged once the default moves again. Short rows are padded with a resolved default cell instead of fixed white-on-black. The `SharedCell` layout is unchanged.
  - **New `ptec_terminal_read_cell_grapheme`** (ARC-101) returns a cell's full UTF-8 grapheme cluster. `SharedCell.text` holds only the base character, so `x` + U+0301 read back as `x`.
  - **Structured events: `on_event_v2`** (ARC-114). A new `TerminalObserverVtable` slot before `user_data` (the vtable grows from 48 to 56 bytes on LP64) receives a `TermEvent { kind, _pad, payload_len, payload }`: one `TERM_EVENT_*` code per event kind, and a length-delimited UTF-8 JSON payload whose keys are the Python event-dict keys. The five text slots still deliver `Debug` text, which remains diagnostic only.
  - **Fixed: text-slot events whose `Debug` text contained a NUL were silently dropped** (`CString::new` failed). The text slots now replace the NUL with U+FFFD, and `on_event_v2` carries it JSON-escaped.
  - Still true from ARC-112: the C surface compiles only with the `ffi` feature, so Python wheels and Rust embedders export no C symbols; C/Swift embedders build `--features rust-only,ffi` (`make xcframework` does).
- **Python per-cell colors follow the palette and the default colors** (ARC-101; **behavior change**). `get_fg_color`, `get_bg_color`, `get_line_cells`, `scrollback_line` and `create_snapshot` resolve through one core resolver, `Terminal::resolve_cell_colors`: the live ANSI palette (OSC 4 / `set_ansi_palette_color`), the OSC 10/11 defaults for default cells, and bold brightening. Only `create_snapshot` used the palette before; the other four returned the fixed xterm table, so SGR 31 read back as (128,0,0) where the snapshot reported `get_ansi_palette()[1]`. On the default theme `create_snapshot` output is unchanged except after OSC 10/11 or `set_default_fg`/`set_default_bg`, which now recolor default cells. `get_underline_color`, `default_fg()` and `default_bg()` also resolve through the palette: `default_fg()` on a fresh terminal returns palette slot 7 (229,229,229) instead of (192,192,192), matching what `get_fg_color` reports for a default cell. Rust gains `Terminal::resolve_cell_colors` / `ResolvedCellColors` beside the existing `Terminal::resolve_color` (the 16-slot palette resolver).
- **The `par-mux` binary needs the new `mux-bin` feature** (ARC-106; **breaking for `cargo install` / `cargo build --bin par-mux`**). `mux` no longer pulls `clap`, so library embedders (par-term) stop compiling it; `mux-bin = ["mux", "clap"]` mirrors the `streaming`/`streaming-bin` split. Install with `cargo install par-term-emu-core-rust --no-default-features --features mux-bin --bin par-mux`. The integration tests that exec the daemon (`mux_cli`, `mux_daemon`, `mux_nested`, `mux_agents`, `mux_hooks`, `mux_restart`) now fail to compile without `mux-bin`: cargo sets `CARGO_BIN_EXE_par-mux` even when the bin is skipped, so a plain-`mux` run would otherwise exec a stale binary. `make check-features` asserts clap is absent from `rust-only,mux`.
- **`Macro` YAML save/load needs the new `macro-yaml` feature** (ARC-116; **breaking for Rust embedders on `rust-only`/`sim`/`mux`**). `Macro::{save_yaml, load_yaml, to_yaml, from_yaml}` are compiled only with `macro-yaml`, which `python`, `python-test` and `streaming-bin` enable, so the Python `Macro` API and `par-term-streamer --macro-file` are unchanged and slim builds no longer compile `serde_yaml_ng`. tokio's `test-util` moves out of the runtime dependency; the dev-dependency already provides it to tests.
- **Wheels have one feature set** (ARC-105): `streaming` moves into `pyproject.toml` `[tool.maturin] features`, so CI, TestPyPI and PyPI wheels and `make dev` builds all include the streaming classes (TestPyPI and CI wheels previously lacked them, and `make dev` silently dropped them from stub regeneration). The per-command `--features streaming` flags are gone; `make dev-streaming` and `make build-streaming` remain as aliases of `make dev` / `make build`. `make dev` now compiles the tokio/axum/rustls stack, so it takes longer.
- **`Cargo.lock` is committed; CI and release builds use `--locked`** (ARC-107). Every `cargo build`/`test`/`clippy` step and every maturin wheel build in the workflows fails instead of re-resolving when the lockfile is stale, and jobs that build through `make` or a script (the feature matrix, FFI drift, xcframework and bench gate) assert it first with `cargo fetch --locked`. Released wheels, streamer binaries and the xcframework are now built from the reviewed dependency set rather than whatever resolved on the day. The `fuzz/` workspace's lockfile stays untracked.
- **Core diagnostics reach the `log` facade** (ARC-111; `src/debug.rs`). Every `debug_error!`/`debug_info!`/`debug_log!`/`debug_trace!` record and `debug::log_*` helper is now emitted through `log` under the target `par_term_emu_core_rust` (`debug::LOG_TARGET`), formatted `[CATEGORY] message`, at the level the installed logger enables. The `DEBUG_LEVEL` temp-file log is now an optional second sink with the same format, except that a `DEBUG_LEVEL=4` buffer snapshot is written as a normal `[ts] [TRACE] [BUFFER_SNAPSHOT] …` record instead of a bare banner block. **Visible effect for embedders:** par-mux's stderr logger (Info and above) now prints core Error/Info records such as `PTY_SPAWN` command lines. `par-term-streamer` prints them through its tracing subscriber, whose `tracing-log` bridge is on by default: Info records by default, and with `--verbose` every Debug record as well, including per-sequence `CSI`/`OSC`/`ESC` dispatch (client keystrokes take the queued raw-writer path and are not logged as `PTY_WRITE`; only `--macro-file` playback is). par-term's log bridge records Info and above to its log file. Filter the `par_term_emu_core_rust` target to silence core records. With no `DEBUG_LEVEL` and no logger, the per-sequence level check is still two atomic loads. Pinned by `tests/debug_log_facade.rs`.
- **`ClientMessage::Mouse.event_type` is a `MouseEventType` enum** (QA-213; `src/streaming/protocol.rs`; **breaking for Rust embedders constructing `ClientMessage::Mouse` or calling `ClientMessage::mouse`**). `Press`/`Release`/`Move`/`Scroll`, with `as_str()`/`parse()` for the wire names. The protobuf wire format is unchanged (`MouseInput.event_type` stays a string); an unknown name decodes as `Press`, as before, when every name but `"release"` counted as a press. The Python dict API still reads and writes the lowercase name and defaults to `"press"`, but an unknown name now comes back as `"press"` instead of verbatim. serde JSON output is unchanged for the four names, and deserializing JSON with any other name is now an error.
- **The 17 internal `impl_terminal_*!` binding macros are no longer exported at the crate root** (ARC-117). They were `#[macro_export]`ed into the public API and docs.rs although they expand to crate-private items and could not be used outside the crate; they are now `pub(crate) use` re-exports from `python_bindings::common`.
- **The Python stub carries docstrings and documented types** (DOC-116; `scripts/generate_stubs.py`, `_native.pyi`). Every class, method, function and property in `_native.pyi` now has its binding docstring for IDE hover help. Return and parameter types come from the Google-style `Returns:`/`Args:` sections where those name a concrete type; the rest stay `Any`.

- **`SpawnContext` gains an `output` sink** (ARC-103; **breaking for Rust embedders**, `src/mux/pane.rs`). A par-mux pane's output sink now rides the spawn context as `Option<&OutputSink>` and `ShellPaneFactory` installs it on the PTY before the child spawns, so the first bytes a new pane prints (`new-session`, `new-window`, `split-window`, `respawn-pane`) reach clients. Before, the sink was installed only after the pane was inserted into the tree, and anything printed in between reached the daemon grid but no client. Struct literals of `SpawnContext` need `output: None` or `..SpawnContext::default()`. A custom factory that builds its own `PtySession` should install `context.output` before spawning; the dispatcher re-installs the sink after the insert, so a factory that ignores it keeps today's early-output gap. With the sink live from the first byte, `%output %N` can precede the command's reply and `%window-add`/`%layout-change`; clients drop output for a pane they have not mapped, as before. The four creation commands now share one `spawn_and_wire` helper instead of four copies of the wiring block.
- **par-mux `new-window`/`new-session` reject trailing command text instead of ignoring it** (audit QA-219; `src/mux/command.rs`). `new-window sleep 5` replied success and opened a shell, silently dropping `sleep 5`. A positional word (or anything after `--`) is now an error pointing at `respawn-pane`. tmux's value flags consume their value (`new-session -x 80 -y 24` still parses), quoted values stay one word, and unknown flags stay tolerated. Neither par-term sender passes a command.
- **Behavior: Python `Terminal.encode_key` option-key modes default to ESC on both sides** (audit ARC-093/QA-189, `src/python_bindings/terminal/input_api.rs`; **breaking for Python callers relying on the old default**). `left_option`/`right_option` defaulted to 0 (Normal passthrough), so Alt+f encoded as `b'f'` from Python but `b'\x1bf'` from C `terminal_encode_key` and Rust `KeyEncodeOptions::default()`, contradicting the documented byte identity. The default is now 2 (ESC prefix); pass `left_option=0, right_option=0` for the old passthrough.
- **par-mux `send-keys` and macro playback encode keys through the shared encoder** (audit ARC-093; `src/mux/command.rs`, `src/mux/dispatch.rs`, `src/macros.rs`, `src/terminal/macros.rs`; **breaking for Rust embedders matching `MuxCommand::SendKeys`**). There were three named-key tables, and neither par-mux nor macros honored the application's modes, so arrows sent to an app in application-cursor mode got `ESC [ A` instead of `ESC O A`.
  - **send-keys**: `Up`/`Down`/`Left`/`Right`, `Home`, `End`, `PageUp`/`PgUp`/`PPage`, `PageDown`/`PgDn`/`NPage`, `Insert`/`IC`, `Delete`/`DC`, `F1`–`F12` and `BTab` are encoded against the target pane's DECCKM and kitty keyboard state, as tmux does. The control-byte names (`C-x`, `C-Space`, `Escape`, `BSpace`, `Space`, `Enter`, and the new `Tab`) stay raw bytes, because par-term sends already-encoded keystrokes in that form and re-encoding them would double-encode. **Behavior change:** `Tab` and every navigation/function name above except the four arrows were written as literal text before and are now keys. Key names match inside quoted runs too, as in tmux, so a par-term keystroke chunk that spells exactly one of these words now sends the key; send literal text with `-l`. `MuxCommand::SendKeys.keys` is now a `SendKeysPayload` of `SendKeysPart::Bytes`/`Key` parts, encoded at dispatch with `SendKeysPayload::encode`; the terminal is read only when a `Key` part is present.
  - **Macros**: `MacroEngine::tick_macro`, Python `PtyTerminal.tick_macro` and `par-term-streamer --macro-file` encode through the new `KeyParser::encode_key(key, &Terminal)`, so replayed keys follow DECCKM, kitty flags and modifyOtherKeys like a real keypress (under kitty flags, `enter` is `CSI 13u`). `KeyParser::parse_key` keeps its signature and encodes against a freshly reset terminal. **Behavior change:** modifiers on named keys now take effect: `alt+f4` is `ESC[1;3S` (was `ESCOS`), `ctrl+alt+del` is `ESC[3;7~`, `shift+enter` is LF, and `ctrl+alt+a` is `ESC ^A` (was `^A`, Alt dropped). Non-ASCII keys (`é`, `alt+é`) are still sent as their literal bytes.
- **par-mux pane telemetry and hook sequence stamps are typed pane fields** (audit ARC-113; `src/mux/pane.rs`, `src/mux/hooks/`, `src/mux/host_probe.rs`). The `agent_telemetry`, `agent_host_telemetry` and `agent_seq_by_source` metadata keys were JSON strings re-parsed on every `list-agents` read and every hook report, under the tree lock. They are now `MuxPane` fields: the hook telemetry's roster token is base64-encoded once when the report is accepted, the host probe's sample is stored typed and rendered to the same canonical JSON at serve time, and the per-source `seq` stamps are a map. Wire bytes are unchanged (pinned by test). The three keys no longer appear in `MuxPane::metadata()`, which only a Rust embedder reading those raw keys would notice. Nothing is persisted differently: all three were already display-only or volatile.
- **`GridSnapshot` lives in the grid layer** (ARC-108; `9709bc4`; `src/grid/snapshot.rs`). It was defined in `terminal::replay_snapshot`, so the grid layer depended on terminal; it moved verbatim (same serde derives and field order — the par-mux on-disk format) and `terminal::replay_snapshot` re-exports it, so every existing path still resolves.
- **The par-mux shutdown save runs off the tree lock** (ARC-119; `4a7b77e`; `src/mux/persist.rs`). `save_off_lock` takes the lock only for the field capture, then serializes, fsyncs and renames without it, so a wedged filesystem during shutdown no longer stalls the reap tick and late client commands; `save_to_with_origin` keeps its signature for direct callers.
- **The par-mux build stamp ignores a git repo that is not this crate's** (ARC-120; `df747eb`; `build.rs`). A crate vendored inside another repository, or a crates.io tarball extracted in one, stamps the source digest (`src-<digest>`) instead of the outer repo's commit.
- **`emit()`'s daemon-unreachable notifications are one explicit arm** (ARC-094; `9329b96`; `src/mux/hooks/report.rs`). The `'_ => String::new()'` wildcard meant a new `TmuxNotification` variant compiled silently and emitted nothing; the never-constructed variants are now listed explicitly, so adding one fails to compile until the emit decision is made.
- **`checkall` runs a non-mutating `lint-check` gate** (ARC-115; `f51472e`; `Makefile`). `cargo fmt --check`, clippy `-D warnings`, `ruff format --check`, `ruff check` and pyright — the gate reports drift instead of rewriting the tree; `make lint` / `make lint-python` remain the fix targets.
### Removed
- **Legacy stringly-typed event methods removed — breaking (Python API)** (`src/python_bindings/terminal/mod.rs`, `src/python_bindings/observer.rs`; DOC-120). `poll_events_legacy()` and `poll_subscribed_events_legacy()`, the 0.50.0 migration bridge, are gone. Use `poll_events()` / `poll_subscribed_events()`, which return native Python types.
- **`Grid::erase_rectangle`** (audit QA-218; **breaking for Rust embedders**, none known). It was reachable only from a `CSI … $ {` arm in the window handler that the CSI router never calls: DECSERA goes to the terminal-level `handle_decsera`. Use `Terminal::erase_rectangle` (selective, what DECSERA and the Python binding use) or `Grid::erase_rectangle_unconditional`. The dead arm is removed too; DECSERA behavior is unchanged.
- **Four unused Python debug helpers** (audit QA-218; `par_term_emu_core_rust.debug`): `log_snapshot`, `log_terminal_state`, `log_textual_event`, `log_get_line_cells_call`. No caller in this repo, par-term-emu-tui-rust or par-term. The five helpers par-term-emu-tui-rust imports stay.

### Deprecated
- **`streaming::Broadcaster`** (ARC-112). `StreamingServer` never used it (clients are managed per session), and it has no users outside its own tests. It will be removed in a future release.
- **`TmuxNotification.name` carrying the `pane-exited` exit code** (QA-190). Read `exit_code` instead; `name` stops carrying the code for `pane-exited` in 0.59.0.
- **`ServerMessage::connected_full`** (QA-212). Use `ServerMessage::connected_builder()`, the single edit site for `Connected` fields, like the other deprecated `connected_*` constructors.

### Fixed
- **`PtyTerminal.paste()` no longer deadlocks on the terminal lock** (QA-221; `src/python_bindings/pty.rs`). It read the bracketed-paste sequences under `terminal.read()` and kept that guard across `PtySession::write`, which takes `terminal.write()` to record input — a read-to-write request on the same non-reentrant parking_lot RwLock, so every paste self-deadlocked before writing a byte. Beyond the deadlock, holding any guard across the blocking PTY write starves the reader thread once the PTY input buffer fills (the child's echo stops draining, the child stops reading, the write never completes). The sequences are now snapshotted under a short read lock and all writes run with no guard held. Pinned by `test_paste_large_content_output_processing_continues` (tests/test_pty.py), which bounds a regressed paste with `faulthandler.dump_traceback_later(exit=True)` because the GIL-starved pytest-timeout watchdog cannot fire.
- **Python `detect_urls`/`detect_file_paths`/`detect_semantic_items` return correct `row`/`col`** (QA-220; `src/python_bindings/terminal/search_api.rs`). The bindings destructured the core's `DetectedItem(text, col, row)` tuples as `(text, row, col)`, so every `DetectedItem.row`/`.col` pair Python saw was swapped. Pinned by `tests/test_detect_items.py`, which asserts positions where row != col.
- **`check_activity()` is no longer a no-op stub** (QA-220; `src/terminal/notification.rs`, `src/terminal/mod.rs`). The body was an empty `if`; with `activity_enabled` set it now queues an `Activity`/`Visual` event when `update_activity()` was called since the previous event, rate-limited to at most one per `activity_threshold` seconds. Rust tests live beside the implementation in `src/terminal/notification.rs`; Python tests in `tests/test_check_activity.py`.
- **A PTY spawn no longer panics on a non-UTF-8 parent environment** (QA-196; `src/pty_session/`). The inherited-env drop read `std::env::vars()`, which panics on a variable whose name or value is not valid Unicode; it now reads `vars_os()` once.
- **RIS keeps every embedder setting** (audit ARC-100, `src/terminal/mod.rs`): the OSC 1337 transfer cap, bold brightening, window position/iconified state and the mouse/inline-image/command/cwd history caps now live in a `HostConfig` that `reset()` carries whole; the conformance level and bell volumes restore to the host-configured baseline (a program's DECSCL/DECSWBV/DECSMBV still resets). Before this, one `ESC c` from any program restored the 50 MiB OSC 1337 transfer cap after an embedder lowered it. Rust gains `Terminal::set_conformance_level`/`set_warning_bell_volume`/`set_margin_bell_volume`; Python `set_conformance_level`/`set_warning_bell_volume`/`set_margin_bell_volume` now set that baseline instead of feeding the escape (`c1_mode` is accepted and ignored, as the DECSCL handler never read it). A classification test (`tests/test_ris_host_config.py`) fails on any new `set_*` until it is declared host config or VT state.
- **Snapshot restore onto a live terminal reports the whole screen dirty** (audit ARC-092, `src/terminal/replay_snapshot.rs`). `restore_from_snapshot` could make the lower-generation grid visible (an alt-screen snapshot restored over a busy primary screen) without syncing the two grids' damage generations, so `dirty_rows_since(g)` for a generation captured before the restore returned no rows and a damage-driven renderer (FFI readback, ENH-025) kept drawing the old screen. Every wholesale change of the visible screen (alt-screen enter/exit, RIS, both snapshot restores) now goes through one `invalidate_visible_screen()` funnel that syncs generations before marking rows. Only Rust embedders restoring onto a live terminal were affected; par-mux and `SnapshotManager` restore onto fresh terminals.
- **Triggers match rows that scroll off the screen before the next scan** (audit ARC-091, `src/terminal/mod.rs`, `src/terminal/trigger.rs`). Pending trigger rows were visible-row indices, and a row that left the screen between scans was dropped unscanned: an `ERROR` line followed by 10 lines in one feed on a 5-row terminal produced no match, so Notify/RunCommand/MarkLine triggers missed matches in bursty output such as build logs, depending on the OS read size. Every Terminal-level region scroll, IL and DL now goes through a tracked wrapper that scans the pending rows about to leave (into scrollback, off a DECSTBM region, off the alternate screen, or off the bottom via SD/RI/IL) before the grid moves them. Only written rows are scanned, each exactly once (the ARC-064 rule). A match found this way reports the visible row the text occupied just before it scrolled, and its actions now run inside `process()`. Also fixed: the wide-character wrap at the bottom row scrolled without moving pending rows, so the wrapped row's pending index pointed at the new blank line and it was never matched.
- **Kitty key encoding reports non-BMP codepoints whole, and unknown keys encode to nothing in both regimes** (audit ARC-098, merges QA-209; `src/keyboard.rs`). The kitty (`CSI u`) encoder held the key code in a `u16`, so Ctrl+U+1D54F encoded as `CSI 54607;5u`; it now reports `CSI 120143;5u`. `TermKey::Unknown` (or any raw key value that is not a `TERM_KEY_*` code) encoded as `CSI 0u` under kitty while producing nothing under legacy; it is now empty under both, matching the "empty output means the key has no encoding" contract. Under kitty, a `Char` whose codepoint is not a Unicode scalar value (e.g. a surrogate) is likewise empty instead of `CSI 0;…u`.
- **`get_stats()` reports non-zero estimates**: `estimated_memory_bytes` and `hyperlink_memory_bytes` were always 0 (QA-206; `src/terminal/metrics.rs`). Both are now computed lower bounds: cells times `size_of::<Cell>()`, and per-URL bytes including the hyperlink map key and `String` header.
- **A reaped child's PID is never signalled again** (audit SEC-125; `src/pty_session/`). A held (remain-on-exit) par-mux pane, or a Python `PtyTerminal` after `try_wait()`, kept its reaped child's PID. Later `kill-pane`/`respawn-pane -k` SIGHUPed it through portable-pty's raw `kill(2)`, and every resize sent SIGWINCH to it and its process group. Once the OS recycled the PID, a same-user process could be terminated. `PtySession` now records the exit under a lock the first time `try_wait`/`wait`/`kill` (or a respawn's cleanup) reaps the child. Every signal path, including the reader thread's alt-screen pulse, checks that record while holding it. As a result, `pane-info cmd=`, the host probe, scrape agent liveness and respawn's cwd fallback no longer read a released PID. **Behavior change:** `PtySession::child_pid()` / Python `child_pid()` return `None` once the exit has been observed. `kill()` after the exit returns `Ok`/`None` instead of raising `OSError` (ESRCH).
- **`respawn-pane` no longer reads flags from inside the command** (audit SEC-126, merges QA-183; `src/mux/command.rs`). The parser scanned the whole line for `-k` and `-c`. So `respawn-pane -t %0 sh -c 'echo X; sort -k 1 f; sleep 600'` killed a live pane without `-k`, took the inner `-c` as the start directory, and ran the fragment `X; sort -k 1 f; sleep 600'`. It still replied success and broadcast `%pane-respawned`. Also fixed: `-c '/a b'` produced the command `b' …`, `-c /tmp -t %0 top` produced `-t %0 top`, and runs of whitespace inside quotes collapsed. Flags (`-t`, `-c`, `-k`, any order) are now read only up to the first command word or `--`, getopt-style. The command is the raw rest of the line, passed to the pane's shell with its quoting and whitespace intact. **Behavior change:** an unknown leading flag (`-x`), a repeated `-t`/`-c`, combined flags (`-kt`), or an empty `-c ''` is now an error. A command starting with `-` must follow `--`.
- **`respawn-pane`'s default cwd no longer trusts a remote OSC 7** (audit SEC-128; `src/mux/pane.rs`, `src/mux/tree/`). With no `-c`, respawn restarted in the pane's OSC 7 cwd. OSC 7 is program output: a remote shell over SSH, or anything printing the escape, chose the local directory the new process ran in. The new `MuxPane::respawn_cwd` uses OSC 7 only when its host is absent, `localhost`, or this machine's hostname (full or short form, case-insensitive), and only when the directory exists. Otherwise it uses the live child's kernel-reported cwd (the `-k` case). Failing both, it defers to the factory's pane cwd, then `$HOME`, where a fresh pane lands. It no longer uses the daemon process's own working directory. A held dead pane has no child cwd (SEC-125), so it never falls back to a reaped PID. This matches SEC-115's rule for the host probe. Persistence/restore still prefers OSC 7 (`persistence_cwd` is unchanged).
- **The par-mux control socket's 1 MiB line budget now holds for a line that never ends** (audit SEC-127, recurring SEC-118; `src/mux/server.rs`). The budget was checked only after `read_line` returned, and `read_line` never returns without a newline, so any same-user program (including one inside a pane via `$PAR_MUX_SOCKET`) could stream bytes with no newline and grow the daemon until it OOMed, taking every session with it (reproduced: 256 MiB in, RSS 12 → 279 MiB, connection still open). The read loop now accumulates raw bytes through a bounded `fill_buf` loop that checks the budget per chunk, so an unterminated stream is answered with the same `%error` block and closed near 1 MiB. The same change fixes a silent data loss: a recv-timeout wake that split a multi-byte UTF-8 character made `read_line` discard the partial it had already consumed, so the rest of the line was answered with `%error`. Bytes now stay raw until the line completes and UTF-8 is decoded once.
- **The par-mux control-command debug summary no longer records typed input or clipboard content** (audit SEC-132, recurring SEC-123; `src/mux/server.rs`, `docs/SECURITY.md`). `summarize_line` kept the first 120 bytes of every control line, so at `DEBUG_LEVEL>=1` (rejected and unparseable lines) and `>=3` (every command) the log captured `send-keys` input, including passwords typed into a pane, and `set-buffer` clipboard content. Those two commands are now logged as name, leading `-t` target and byte count only, whether or not the line parses. `set-environment` and `new-session -e` values are still logged verbatim, and at `DEBUG_LEVEL>=3` the PTY write log still hex-dumps every byte delivered to a pane; SECURITY.md gains a "Debug Logging" subsection that says both.
- **The par-mux host probe can no longer deadlock on git output or hang the daemon on a wedged filesystem** (audit SEC-133, merges QA-193, recurring SEC-124 + QA-155; `src/mux/host_probe.rs`, `src/mux/server.rs`). `run_git` read stdout only after git exited, so a repo whose `ls-files --others` output passed the 64 KiB pipe buffer blocked git until the 5 s kill and `git_dirty` served nothing. Each pane's `statvfs` and three git runs had no combined bound, so one sweep could take 25 s, and shutdown joined it unconditionally, so a hung NFS/FUSE mount could block `par-mux --stop` forever. Now: stdout is drained on its own thread and capped per call (1 byte for the dirty check, just past the branch limit for the branch), so a chatty git fails its next write instead of blocking; killed children are reaped on a detached thread; each pane's probe runs on a detached worker the sweep waits for at most 6 s, and a pane whose probe timed out is skipped until its cwd changes; the shutdown flag is checked before each pane and each git run; and the daemon waits at most 1 s for the probe thread at shutdown before detaching it. At most 4 probe threads may be outstanding before the sweep stops starting new ones.
- **par-mux `resize-pane` while zoomed no longer rewrites the hidden layout** (audit ARC-090; `src/mux/tree/`). `resize-pane -x/-y/-L/-R/-U/-D` never cleared the zoom, so it moved the divider behind a zoomed pane. Splitting an 80-column window 40/40, zooming `%0`, sending `resize-pane -x 79` and unzooming restored a 79/1 layout. par-term sends this on every divider drag. A successful resize now ends the zoom first, as tmux does (`server_unzoom_window`). A rejected resize (no divider on that axis) changes nothing, including the zoom. `-x` and `-y` together are all-or-nothing. Before, when one axis moved and the other spanned the window, the first change stayed applied and the terminals were never re-fitted. Every layout mutation (split, swap, break, join, kill, resize) now goes through one `MuxTree::mutate_layout` choke point that ends the zoom and re-fits the panes, instead of seven hand-written `zoomed = None` sites. A same-window `join-pane` now re-fits each pane once, to the final layout. **Behavior change for par-term:** a divider drag in a zoomed par-mux window now unzooms it.
- **par-mux `respawn-pane -k` no longer leaks the dying process's output into the respawned pane** (audit ARC-089; `src/mux/pane.rs`, `src/mux/tree/`, `src/mux/dispatch.rs`). The replacement reuses the pane id. The old process kept its `%output` sink through portable-pty's SIGHUP grace and the reap, so a SIGHUP handler's parting bytes arrived as `%output %N` beside `%pane-respawned %N`. A TUI's alt-screen exit and cursor restore landed on the fresh screen in every client. The new `MuxPane::detach_output` stops forwarding and returns only once no sink call is in flight. It runs synchronously before every detached kill (`kill-pane`, `kill-window`, `kill-session`, respawn and failed spawns), inside `MuxPane::kill`, and for `respawn-pane -k` before the off-lock spawn. If that spawn fails, the still-live pane resumes forwarding. The killed process's exit output is no longer broadcast, including to a `kill-pane` id that no longer exists. While a `-k` spawn runs, the old process's bytes still reach the daemon's terminal but not clients. After a failed `-k`, a mirror rebuilt from `%output` lacks them.
- **par-mux `refresh-client` sizes are capped, and pane pixel extents no longer overflow** (audit QA-182; `src/mux/command.rs`, `src/mux/pane.rs`, `src/pty_session/`, `src/python_bindings/pty.rs`). `-C` is capped at 1000×500 (the streaming server's viewer bounds, so a mux-backed streamer's forwarded resize is never refused) and `-p` at 512×512 per cell; a larger report is an error. Before, any size up to 65535×65535 was accepted, and a grid that size could abort the daemon on allocation. Separately, `cols * cell_w` was computed in `u16`, so 2000 columns of 40 px cells panicked; PTY pixel extents now saturate at `u16::MAX`. Python `PtyTerminal.resize`/`resize_pixels` raise `ValueError` for a value past 65535 instead of silently truncating it.
- **`PtyTerminal` methods that mutate the terminal from Python, and par-mux restores and client-color reports, now republish the geometry mirror** (audit QA-195; `src/pty_session/`, `src/python_bindings/pty.rs`, `src/mux/pane.rs`, `src/mux/tree/`, `src/mux/persist.rs`). They wrote through the raw terminal lock, which skipped the wait-free `(cols, rows, cursor)` mirror, so `cursor_position()`/`size()` stayed stale until the next PTY output. They now go through `PtySession::terminal_write()` or `MuxPane::with_terminal_mut`, which publish on release.
- **The streaming server's input/write paths are load-hardened** (QA-191 `4b46b55`, QA-192 `60b67f3`, QA-198 `bec391c`, QA-184 `63835bc`; `src/streaming/session.rs`, `src/streaming/server.rs`). Input on a writer-less session is dropped and counted instead of closing the WebSocket (the bundled frontend's retry looped forever); the PTY input drain parks in `recv_timeout` instead of polling at ~100 wakes/s and bounds its writer wait; blocking PTY and daemon writes move off tokio workers via `spawn_blocking`; mux session creation gets a per-reply deadline instead of unbounded blocking on the WebSocket accept path.
- **Python PTY writes release the GIL while blocked** (QA-224; `9cdb74e`; `src/python_bindings/pty.rs`). `paste`/`write`/`write_str`/`tick_macro` held the GIL across `PtySession::write`, which can block on a full PTY buffer, starving every other Python thread; all four route through one `py.detach` helper.
- **par-mux operational fixes**: `paste-buffer` snapshots and writes off the tree mutex (QA-225; `a93f167`); the stale-socket probe re-probes for 150 ms before refusing a teardown ghost (QA-223; `7ffdb35`); a failed pane resize is logged instead of silently discarded (QA-188; `c703ad9`).
- **Restored panes clamp hostile persisted geometry** (`9135598`, `665ba2f`; the ARC-113 restore-hardening family; `src/mux/persist.rs`). A state file claiming a 65535×65535 window or degenerate grid dimensions now clamps to the streaming-layer bounds (2..=1000 cols, 1..=500 rows) instead of driving the allocation.
- **Production `unwrap()`s removed from server, file-transfer, macro and export paths** (QA-216; `145a31c`).
- **Every production unsafe block states its invariant, and undocumented ones are linted** (QA-201; `7952272`; `#![cfg_attr(not(test), warn(clippy::undocumented_unsafe_blocks))]`).
- **`SharedState.cell_count` derives from its allocation** (QA-215; `7a20994`), removing the duplicated count `Drop` rebuilt the slice from.
- **The Windows `TOKEN_USER` query buffer is correctly aligned** (QA-194; `f9b489d`): a `Vec<u64>` sized in 8-byte units instead of a `Vec<u8>` viewed as a pointer-bearing struct.
- **The unneeded unsafe `Send`/`Sync` impls on the Python observer wrappers are gone** (QA-210; `0cc12dd`) — pyo3 already implements them for `Py<T>`.
- **Dead `TerminalDebug.tsx` removed** (QA-211; `55359f8`): nothing imported it and it carried an ungated `console.log`.
- **Color and attribute tests assert real palette values** (QA-217; `cd3bf92`) instead of `is not None`.
- **The `check_mux_docs` cfg(test) stripper skips string and char literals** (QA-229; `b1e482d`), so a test fixture with unbalanced braces can no longer shift the cut point.
- **Unix-only PTY reader bindings are cfg-gated for Windows `-D warnings`** (`914ac15`).
- **`check_release_notes.py` no longer derives its argparse description from `__doc__`** (`97d0290`), which pyright flags as `str | None`.



## [0.57.0] - 2026-09-29

### Added
- **par-mux pane zoom** (`b42e1e0`): `resize-pane -Z` zooms a pane to fill its window and restores the exact prior layout on unzoom; zoom state broadcasts `%layout-change` with the zoomed layout.
- **par-mux pane move family** (`37be711`): `break-pane`, `join-pane`, `move-window`, and `swap-window` — panes and windows can be reorganized across sessions/windows, closing the gap with tmux's layout commands. Wire tests and `docs/MUX.md` rows included.
- **par-mux dead-pane hold and respawn** (`4437667`, `0b58dc3`, `4dde9e5`): a pane whose child exits is held with its exit code (remain-on-exit semantics) instead of being torn down immediately; `respawn-pane -t %N [-k] [-c dir] [command]` replaces the command in a live or held pane. `%pane-exited`/`%pane-respawned` notifications are mapped in the Python `TmuxNotification` converter.
- **par-mux `split-window -b` and a `pane-info` foreground-command token** (`1c4c479`): `split-window -b` places the new pane before its target — left of it under `-h`, above it in the default direction — keeping exactly its `-p` share. `pane-info` replies append an optional last `cmd=<base64>` token (standard base64 of the pane's foreground command name, tmux `#{pane_current_command}`), absent on Windows or when the process argv is unreadable. The fixed `%N @W COLSxROWS` prefix is unchanged, so existing parsers keep working.

### Changed
- **ENH-026: allocation-free FFI dirty ranges** (`bab1b27`): `terminal_dirty_ranges` coalesces runs in place on the caller-owned buffer instead of building an intermediate allocation, cutting per-frame readback cost.
- **ENH-031: pre-sized nibble-table hex encoder** (`d827666`): the streaming `send_keys_line` hex path no longer formats through the generic formatter.
- **ENH-029: typed telemetry schema** (`fa03f60`): the par-mux telemetry validator is replaced by a serde-typed `TelemetryV1` schema, deleting the hand-rolled field-by-field validator.
- **ENH-032: third-party GitHub Actions pinned to commit SHAs** (`5740f90`, `1f0b446`) across all workflows.

### Fixed
- **QA-156: par-mux rejects future-dated telemetry** (`aa6286b`): `sampled_at_unix_ms` ahead of the daemon clock is rejected instead of poisoning the agent roster.
- **Real constructor signatures in the stub, and constructor/property coverage in the API_REFERENCE checker** (ENH-030; `scripts/generate_stubs.py`, `scripts/check_api_reference.py`). PyO3 0.29 sets the class-level `__text_signature__` from `#[new]`/`#[pyo3(signature=…)]`, so the stub generator now renders every `__init__` from it — all 13 `*args/**kwargs` constructors became real signatures, with the opaque form kept only as a fallback for classes exposing no signature. The generator refuses to run against a non-streaming build (placeholder `StreamingConfig` has a `'()'` signature) with a message pointing at `make dev-streaming`. `check_api_reference.py` now also validates the first fenced `ClassName(args)` block or bullet per class section against the stub's `__init__` (names + default presence, DOC-064 rules), and checks `### Properties` sections bidirectionally — documented names must be stub properties and every stub property must be documented (empty escape-hatch allowlist). First catch: `StreamingConfig.kitty_file_media` was missing from both the constructor signature and the Properties section (audit DOC-073) — documented now with its SEC-101 semantics.
- **The shared key encoder now covers modifyOtherKeys and the macOS option-key modes** (ENH-028; `src/keyboard.rs`, `src/ffi.rs`, `src/python_bindings/terminal/input_api.rs`). `keyboard::encode_key` grows into the single encoder par-term, ParDeck and Python all call, replacing the modes that previously lived only in par-term's `key_encoding.rs`: text keys with Ctrl or Alt held encode as `CSI 27;mods;codepoint~` under modifyOtherKeys mode 1/2 (one rule set, matching par-term exactly — Shift-only exempt, ASCII bases only), and `encode_key_with(ev, term, &KeyEncodeOptions{left_option, right_option})` selects Normal/Meta/Esc option-key handling per side, picked by the new `modifiers::ALT_RIGHT` side bit (bit 6, above the kitty-order bits; ignored by every parameter field). Also par-term parity: Shift+Enter sends LF, and the legacy modifier parameter counts Shift/Alt/Ctrl only, so Super held alone no longer fabricates `CSI 1;N` parameters. Defaults (ESC both sides) keep `encode_key`/`terminal_encode_key` byte-identical to the previous wire behavior. New surface: FFI `terminal_encode_key_ex` + `TermKeyOptions` (Rust `KeyEncodeOptions`) (TERM_OPTION_MODE_* constants, ABI 3); Python `Terminal.encode_key(key, modifiers, codepoint=0, left_option=0, right_option=0)`. Data-driven conformance tests derive from par-term-input's suite (~360 rows plus a 1350-case no-truncation sweep), red-proofed against the old encoder first; two deliberate divergences are noted in the test module (Home/End keep SS3 under DECCKM, Alt+Space runs the option transform). Kitty protocol levels 2-4 remain out of scope (level 1 unchanged, pinned by tests).

## [0.56.0] - 2026-09-29

### Removed (breaking for Rust embedders)
- **The 29 deprecated `Terminal` macro/trigger forwarding methods are gone** (pinned by 0.54.0's deprecation schedule, removed on it).
